is geodesic, i.e.,
\mathscr{D}_{{\tilde{L}}}{\tilde{L}}=0
. Then it holds that

{\tilde{L}}^{\alpha}(\partial_{\alpha}{\tilde{L}}^{\beta})\partial_{\beta}+{\tilde{L}}^{\alpha}{\tilde{L}}^{\beta}\Gamma_{\alpha\beta}^{\gamma}\partial_{\gamma}=0,

(4.87)

where
\Gamma_{\alpha\beta}^{\gamma}
are Christoffel symbols with

\begin{split}\Gamma_{\alpha\beta}^{\gamma}=\frac{1}{2}g^{\gamma\kappa}\big(F_{\kappa\beta}\partial_{\alpha}\phi+G_{\kappa\beta}^{\nu}\partial_{\alpha}\varphi_{\nu}+F_{\alpha\kappa}\partial_{\beta}\phi+G_{\alpha\kappa}^{\nu}\partial_{\beta}\varphi_{\nu}-F_{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\kappa}\phi-G_{\alpha\beta}^{\nu}\partial_{\kappa}\varphi_{\nu}\big).\end{split}

By
\tilde{L}^{0}=\mu^{-1}
and
\mathring{L}=\mu\tilde{L}
, taking the component relative to
x^{0}
in (
4.87
) yields

\begin{split}\mathring{L}\mu=&\mu\mathring{L}^{\alpha}\mathring{L}^{\gamma}\Gamma_{\alpha\gamma}^{0}\\
=&-\frac{1}{2}\mu\big\{F_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}\mathring{L}\phi+G_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}^{\gamma}\mathring{L}\varphi_{\gamma}+2F_{\tilde{T}\mathring{L}}\mathring{L}\phi+2G_{\tilde{T}\mathring{L}}^{\gamma}\mathring{L}\varphi_{\gamma}\big\}\\
&+\frac{1}{2}F_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}T\phi+\frac{1}{2}G_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}^{\gamma}T\varphi_{\gamma},\end{split}

(4.88)

here the fact
g^{0\kappa}=-\mathring{L}^{\kappa}-\tilde{T}^{\kappa}
has been used.
∎

Remark 4.3
.

The important role played by (
4.86
) should be emphasized here. Since the first null condition holds in (
1.10
),
the term
\frac{1}{2}G_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}^{\gamma}T\varphi_{\gamma}
in (
4.86
) will admit the better smallness and
faster time-decay rate
than the ones of the single factor
T\varphi_{\gamma}
. This will imply

|\mathring{L}\mu|\lesssim M\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-5/2}\mu+M^{2}\delta^{1-2\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-3/2}

and further yield
\mu\geq C
for some positive constant
C
(see (
5.127
)
in Section
5
). Therefore, this is in sharp contrast to the case in
[
9
]
,
[
36
]
and
[
39
]
,
where the expressions of
\mathring{L}\mu

contain the pure “bad” factor
T\varphi_{\alpha}
which leads to
\mu\rightarrow 0+
in finite time (e.g. (2.36) in
[
36
]
).

Since the quantity “deformation tensor” defined in (
4.79
)
will occur in the subsequent energy estimates, it is necessary to compute the components
of
{\vphantom{\pi}}{}^{(V)}\pi
in the null frame
\{\mathring{L},\underline{\mathring{L}},X_{1},X_{2},X_{3}\}
.

Set
{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(V)}\not{\pi}_{UA}:={\vphantom{\pi}}^{(V)}\pi_{UX_{A}}
for
U\in\{\mathring{L},\underline{\mathring{L}},X_{1},X_{2},X_{3}\}
.
By referring to the components in Proposition 7.7 of
[
39
]
, one has

(1)

when
V=T
,

\begin{split}&{\vphantom{\pi}}{}^{(T)}\pi_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}=0,\quad{\vphantom{\pi}}^{(T)}\pi_{T\tilde{T}}=2T\mu,\quad{\vphantom{\pi}}^{(T)}\pi_{\mathring{L}T}=-T\mu,\quad{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}^{(T)}\not{\pi}_{TA}=0,\\
&{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(T)}\not{\pi}_{\mathring{L}A}=-2\mu\zeta_{A}-\not{d}_{A}\mu,\quad{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}^{(T)}\not{\pi}_{AB}=2\mu\sigma_{AB};\end{split}

(4.89)

(2)

when
V=\mathring{L}
,

\begin{split}&{\vphantom{\pi}}{}^{(\mathring{L})}\pi_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}=0,\quad{\vphantom{\pi}}^{(\mathring{L})}\pi_{T\tilde{T}}=2\mathring{L}\mu,\quad{\vphantom{\pi}}^{(\mathring{L})}\pi_{\mathring{L}T}=-\mathring{L}\mu,\quad{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}^{(\mathring{L})}\not{\pi}_{\mathring{L}A}=0,\\
&{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(\mathring{L})}\not{\pi}_{TA}=2\mu\zeta_{A}+\not{d}_{A}\mu,\quad{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}^{(\mathring{L})}\not{\pi}_{AB}=2\chi_{AB};\end{split}

(4.90)

(3)

when
V=R_{ij}
,

\begin{split}&{\vphantom{\pi}}{}^{(R_{ij})}\pi_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}=0,\quad{\vphantom{\pi}}^{(R_{ij})}\pi_{T\tilde{T}}=2R_{ij}\mu,\quad{\vphantom{\pi}}^{(R_{ij})}\pi_{\mathring{L}T}=-R_{ij}\mu,\\
&{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(R_{ij})}\not{\pi}_{\mathring{L}A}=-{R_{ij}}^{B}\check{\chi}_{AB}+\frac{1}{2}{R_{ij}}^{B}\{(\mathcal{FG})_{AB}({\mathring{L}})-(\mathcal{FG})_{\mathring{L}B}({X_{A}})\}\\
&\qquad\qquad\quad-\upsilon_{ij}\{(\mathcal{FG})_{\mathring{L}\tilde{T}}({X_{A}})+\frac{1}{2}(\mathcal{FG})_{\tilde{T}\tilde{T}}({X_{A}})-(\mathcal{FG})_{A\tilde{T}}({\mathring{L}})\}\\
&\qquad\qquad\quad+\frac{1}{2}(\mathcal{FG})_{\mathring{L}A}({R_{ij}})+g_{ja}\check{L}^{i}\not{d}_{A}x^{a}-g_{ia}\check{L}^{j}\not{d}_{A}x^{a},\\
&{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(R_{ij})}\not{\pi}_{TA}=\mu{R_{ij}}^{B}\check{\chi}_{AB}+\upsilon_{ij}\not{d}_{A}\mu+\frac{1}{2}\mu{R_{ij}}^{B}\{(\mathcal{FG})_{B\mathring{L}}({X_{A}})-(\mathcal{FG})_{AB}({\mathring{L}})\}\\
&\qquad\qquad\quad+\frac{1}{2}\mu(\mathcal{FG})_{A\mathring{L}}({R_{ij}})+\mu(\mathcal{FG})_{A\tilde{T}}({R_{ij}})+\upsilon_{ij}\{(\mathcal{FG})_{A\tilde{T}}(T)\\
&\qquad\qquad\quad-\frac{1}{2}\mu(\mathcal{FG})_{\tilde{T}\tilde{T}}(X_{A})\}+\mu g_{ja}\check{T}^{i}\not{d}_{A}x^{a}-\mu g_{ia}\check{T}^{j}\not{d}_{A}x^{a},\\
&{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(R_{ij})}\not{\pi}_{AB}=2\upsilon_{ij}\chi_{AB}+\upsilon_{ij}\{(\mathcal{FG})_{B\mathring{L}}({X_{A}})+(\mathcal{FG})_{B\tilde{T}}({X_{A}})+(\mathcal{FG})_{A\mathring{L}}({X_{B}})\\
&\qquad\qquad\quad+(\mathcal{FG})_{A\tilde{T}}({X_{B}})-(\mathcal{FG})_{AB}({\mathring{L}})\}+(\mathcal{FG})_{AB}({R_{ij}})+\check{g}_{ja}(\not{d}_{A}x^{i})\not{d}_{B}x^{a}\\
&\qquad\qquad\quad-\check{g}_{ia}(\not{d}_{A}x^{j})\not{d}_{B}x^{a}+\check{g}_{ja}(\not{d}_{B}x^{i})\not{d}_{A}x^{a}-\check{g}_{ia}(\not{d}_{B}x^{j})\not{d}_{A}x^{a},\end{split}

(4.91)

where
\check{g}_{ia}:=g_{ia}-m_{ia}
, the definitions of
\tilde{T}
and
\check{T}^{i}
,
\check{L}^{i}
are given in (
4.69
)
and (
4.80
) respectively.

It follows from the equation (
4.86
) and (
4.89
)-(
4.91
) that
the vector fields
\mathring{L}
and
T
appear frequently. In view of
\tilde{T}^{i}=-g^{0i}-\mathring{L}^{i}

and
T=\mu\tilde{T}
,
it is important to derive the equations for
\mathring{L}^{i}
and
\check{L}^{i}
in the null frame
\{T,\mathring{L},X_{1},X_{2},X_{3}\}
.

Lemma 4.4
.

It holds that

\displaystyle\mathring{L}\mathring{L}^{i}=\frac{1}{2}(\mathcal{FG})_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}({\mathring{L}})\tilde{T}^{i}-\big\{(\mathcal{FG})_{A\mathring{L}}({\mathring{L}})-\frac{1}{2}(\mathcal{FG})_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}({X_{A}})\big\}\not{d}^{A}x^{i},

(4.92)

\displaystyle\mathring{L}\big(\varrho\check{L}^{i}\big)=\varrho\mathring{L}\mathring{L}^{i},

(4.93)

\displaystyle\not{d}_{A}\mathring{L}^{i}=\chi_{AB}{\not{d}^{B}}x^{i}-\{(\mathcal{FG})_{\mathring{L}\tilde{T}}({X_{A}})+\frac{1}{2}(\mathcal{FG})_{\tilde{T}\tilde{T}}({X_{A}})\}\tilde{T}^{i}+\Lambda_{AB}\not{d}^{B}x^{i},

(4.94)

\displaystyle\not{d}_{A}\check{L}^{i}=\check{\chi}_{AB}{\not{d}^{B}}x^{i}-\{(\mathcal{FG})_{\mathring{L}\tilde{T}}({X_{A}})+\frac{1}{2}(\mathcal{FG})_{\tilde{T}\tilde{T}}({X_{A}})\}\tilde{T}^{i}+\Lambda_{AB}\not{d}^{B}x^{i},

(4.95)

where

\Lambda_{AB}:=-\frac{1}{2}(\mathcal{FG})_{B\mathring{L}}({X_{A}})-\frac{1}{2}(\mathcal{FG})_{AB}({\mathring{L}})+\frac{1}{2}(\mathcal{FG})_{A\mathring{L}}({X_{B}}).

(4.96)

Proof.

(
4.92
)-(
4.95
) follow directly from Proposition 4.7 in
[
39
]
and (
4.84
).
∎

With the help of Lemma
4.4
, following the proof of Lemma 5.1 in
[
39
]
, one can
get the explicit expressions of
\zeta_{A}
and
\sigma_{AB}
as

\displaystyle\zeta_{A}=-\mu^{-1}\not{d}_{A}\mu+\mu^{-1}\eta_{A}

\displaystyle\quad=-\frac{1}{2}\Big\{(\mathcal{FG})_{\tilde{T}\mathring{L}}({X_{A}})+(\mathcal{FG})_{\tilde{T}\tilde{T}}({X_{A}})-(\mathcal{FG})_{A\tilde{T}}({\mathring{L}})+(\mathcal{FG})_{A\mathring{L}}({\tilde{T}})\Big\},

(4.97)

\displaystyle\sigma_{AB}=-\frac{1}{2}\Big\{(\mathcal{FG})_{B\mathring{L}}({X_{A}})+(\mathcal{FG})_{B\tilde{T}}({X_{A}})+(\mathcal{FG})_{A\mathring{L}}({X_{B}})+(\mathcal{FG})_{A\tilde{T}}({X_{B}})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-(\mathcal{FG})_{AB}({\mathring{L}})-(\mathcal{FG})_{AB}({\tilde{T}})\Big\}-\chi_{AB}.

(4.98)

Then it is deduced from (
4.97
) that

\begin{split}&T\mathring{L}^{i}=\{\not{d}_{A}\mu-\frac{1}{2}\mu(\mathcal{FG})_{\tilde{T}\tilde{T}}({X_{A}})-(\mathcal{FG})_{A\mathring{L}}(T)\}\not{d}^{A}x^{i}+\big\{\frac{1}{2}\mu(\mathcal{FG})_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}({\mathring{L}})\\
&\qquad\quad+\mu(\mathcal{FG})_{\tilde{T}\mathring{L}}({\mathring{L}})+\frac{1}{2}\mu(\mathcal{FG})_{\tilde{T}\tilde{T}}({\mathring{L}})\big\}\mathring{L}^{i}+\frac{1}{2}(\mathcal{FG})_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}(T)\tilde{T}^{i}.\end{split}

(4.99)

For later use, one also needs to give the connection coefficients of the related frames.
This can be derived from Lemma 5.1 and Lemma 5.3 of
[
39
]
.

Lemma 4.5
.

The covariant derivatives in the frame
\{T,\mathring{L},X_{1},X_{2},X_{3}\}
are

\begin{split}&\mathscr{D}_{\mathring{L}}\mathring{L}=(\mu^{-1}\mathring{L}\mu)\mathring{L},\quad\mathscr{D}_{T}\mathring{L}=-\mathring{L}\mu\mathring{L}+\eta^{A}X_{A},\quad\mathscr{D}_{A}\mathring{L}=-\zeta_{A}\mathring{L}+{\chi_{A}}^{B}X_{B},\\
&\mathscr{D}_{\mathring{L}}T=-\mathring{L}\mu\mathring{L}-\mu\zeta^{A}X_{A},\quad\mathscr{D}_{T}T=\mu\mathring{L}\mu\mathring{L}+(\mu^{-1}T\mu+\mathring{L}\mu)T-\mu(\not{d}^{A}\mu)X_{A},\\
&\mathscr{D}_{A}T=\mu\zeta_{A}\mathring{L}+\mu^{-1}\eta_{A}T+\mu{\sigma_{A}}^{B}X_{B},\\
&\mathscr{D}_{A}X_{B}=\not{\nabla}_{A}X_{B}+(\sigma_{AB}+\chi_{AB})\mathring{L}+\mu^{-1}\chi_{AB}T.\end{split}

(4.100)

Lemma 4.6
.

The covariant derivatives in the frame
\{\mathring{\underline{L}},\mathring{L},X_{1},X_{2},X_{3}\}
are

\begin{split}&\mathscr{D}_{\mathring{\underline{L}}}{\mathring{L}}=-\mathring{L}\mu\mathring{L}+2\eta^{A}X_{A},\quad\mathscr{D}_{\mathring{L}}\mathring{\underline{L}}=-2\mu\zeta^{A}X_{A},\\
&\mathscr{D}_{\mathring{\underline{L}}}\mathring{\underline{L}}=(\mu^{-1}\mathring{\underline{L}}\mu+\mathring{L}\mu)\mathring{\underline{L}}-(2\mu\not{d}^{A}\mu)X_{A}.\end{split}

(4.101)

In order to estimate
\varphi_{\gamma}
and
\phi
, by the energy method, we need to
derive the equations for
\phi
and
\varphi_{\gamma}
under the action of the covariant wave operator
\Box_{g}=g^{\alpha\beta}\mathscr{D}^{2}_{\alpha\beta}=g^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\alpha\beta}^{2}-g^{\alpha\beta}\Gamma_{\alpha\beta}^{\lambda}\partial_{\lambda}
.

(1)

For any
\gamma\in\{0,1,2,3,4\}
,
taking the derivative
\partial_{x^{\gamma}}
on two sides of (
1.10
) yields

\begin{split}g^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\alpha\beta}^{2}\varphi_{\gamma}=-\big\{(\partial_{\phi}g^{\alpha\beta})\partial_{\gamma}\phi+(\partial_{\varphi_{\lambda}}g^{\alpha\beta})\partial_{\lambda}\varphi_{\gamma}\big\}\partial_{\alpha\beta}^{2}\phi.\end{split}

(4.102)

Note that

g^{\alpha\beta}g^{\lambda\kappa}(\partial_{\phi}g_{\kappa\beta})=-\partial_{\phi}g^{\alpha\lambda},\quad g^{\alpha\beta}g^{\lambda\kappa}(\partial_{\varphi_{\gamma}}g_{\kappa\beta})=-\partial_{\varphi_{\gamma}}g^{\alpha\lambda}.

(4.103)

Then

\begin{split}-g^{\alpha\beta}\Gamma_{\alpha\beta}^{\lambda}\partial_{\lambda}\varphi_{\gamma}=&(\partial_{\phi}g^{\alpha\beta})\partial_{\alpha}\phi\partial_{\gamma}\varphi_{\beta}+(\partial_{\varphi_{\kappa}}g^{\alpha\beta})\partial_{\kappa}\varphi_{\alpha}\partial_{\beta}\varphi_{\gamma}\\
&+\frac{1}{2}(g^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\phi}g_{\alpha\beta})g^{\lambda\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}\phi\partial_{\lambda}\varphi_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2}(g^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\varphi_{\nu}}g_{\alpha\beta})g^{\lambda\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}\varphi_{\nu}\partial_{\lambda}\varphi_{\gamma}.\end{split}

(4.104)

Thus,

\begin{split}\Box_{g}\varphi_{\gamma}=&\partial_{\phi}g^{\alpha\beta}\big(-\partial_{\gamma}\phi\partial_{\alpha}\varphi_{\beta}+\partial_{\alpha}\phi\partial_{\gamma}\varphi_{\beta}\big)+\partial_{\varphi_{\nu}}g^{\alpha\beta}\big(-\partial_{\nu}\varphi_{\gamma}\partial_{\beta}\varphi_{\alpha}+\partial_{\nu}\varphi_{\alpha}\partial_{\beta}\varphi_{\gamma}\big)\\
&+\frac{1}{2}(g^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\phi}g_{\alpha\beta})g^{\lambda\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}\phi\partial_{\lambda}\varphi_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2}(g^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\varphi_{\nu}}g_{\alpha\beta})g^{\lambda\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}\varphi_{\nu}\partial_{\lambda}\varphi_{\gamma}.\end{split}

(4.105)

In addition, it holds that

\displaystyle g^{\alpha\beta}=-\mathring{L}^{\alpha}\mathring{L}^{\beta}-\tilde{T}^{\alpha}\mathring{L}^{\beta}-\mathring{L}^{\alpha}\tilde{T}^{\beta}+\not{g}^{AB}(\not{d}_{A}x^{\alpha})(\not{d}_{B}x^{\beta}),

(4.106)

\displaystyle\partial_{\alpha}=\delta_{\alpha}^{0}\mathring{L}-\mu^{-1}\mathring{L}_{\alpha}T+g_{\alpha i}(\not{d}^{A}x^{i})X_{A}.

(4.107)

Then in the frame
\{T,\mathring{L},X_{1},X_{2},X_{3}\}
, (
4.105
) can be rewritten as

\begin{split}\mu\Box_{g}\varphi_{\gamma}=&\frac{1}{2}(g^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\phi}g_{\alpha\beta})(\mu\not{d}^{A}\phi)\not{d}_{A}\varphi_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2}(g^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\varphi_{\nu}}g_{\alpha\beta})(\mu\not{d}^{A}\varphi_{\nu})\not{d}_{A}\varphi_{\gamma}\\
&+f(\phi,\varphi,\mathring{L}^{i})\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathring{L}\phi\\
(\not{d}_{A}x^{j})\not{d}^{A}\phi\\
\mathring{L}\varphi\\
(\not{d}_{A}x^{j})\not{d}^{A}\varphi\\
\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}T\phi\\
T\varphi\\
\mu\mathring{L}\varphi\\
\mu(\not{d}_{B}x^{k})\not{d}^{B}\varphi\\
\end{array}\right),\end{split}

(4.108)

where and below
f(\phi,\varphi,\mathring{L}^{i})
represents the generic smooth function with respect to

\phi
,
\varphi
,
\mathring{L}^{1}
,
\mathring{L}^{2}
,
\mathring{L}^{3}
and
\mathring{L}^{4}
,

f(\phi,\varphi,\mathring{L}^{i})\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}A_{1}\\
\vdots\\
A_{n}\\
\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}B_{1}\\
\vdots\\
B_{m}\\
\end{array}\right)

stands for
\sum_{1\leq k\leq n,1\leq l\leq m}f_{kl}(\phi,\varphi,\mathring{L}^{i})A_{k}B_{l}
.

An important observation here is that on the right hand side of (
4.108
), the worst factor
T\varphi
is always accompanied by
the “good” multipliers such as
\mathring{L}\phi
,
\mathring{L}\varphi
,
\not{d}\phi
and
\not{d}\varphi

which admit higher time-decay rates or orders of smallness.

(2)

For the solution
\phi
to (
1.10
), it holds that

\Box_{g}\phi=(\partial_{\alpha}g^{\alpha\lambda})\varphi_{\lambda}+\frac{1}{2}\big(g^{\alpha\beta}F_{\alpha\beta}\big)\big(g^{\kappa\lambda}\varphi_{\kappa}\varphi_{\lambda}\big)+\frac{1}{2}\big(g^{\alpha\beta}G_{\alpha\beta}^{\gamma}\big)\big(g^{\kappa\lambda}\varphi_{\lambda}\partial_{\kappa}\varphi_{\gamma}\big).

(4.109)

Next one can treat each term on the right hand side of (
4.109
) as follows.

(a) Note that

\begin{split}(\partial_{\alpha}g^{\alpha\lambda})\varphi_{\lambda}=&(\mathring{L}g^{0\gamma})\varphi_{\gamma}-\mu^{-1}\mathring{L}_{\alpha}(Tg^{\alpha\lambda})\varphi_{\lambda}+g_{\alpha i}\not{d}^{A}x^{i}(\not{d}_{A}g^{\alpha\lambda})\varphi_{\lambda}\\
=&(\mathring{L}g^{0\gamma})\varphi_{\gamma}-\mu^{-1}\mathring{L}_{\alpha}(Tg^{\alpha\lambda})\varphi_{\lambda}-g^{\alpha\lambda}\not{d}^{A}x^{i}(\not{d}_{A}g_{\alpha i})\varphi_{\lambda}\end{split}

(4.110)

Due to (
4.107
). In addition, it follows from (
4.103
) and (
4.106
) that

\begin{split}&(\mathring{L}g^{0\gamma})\varphi_{\gamma}=\big\{-(g^{0\beta}g^{\gamma\kappa}F_{\kappa\beta})\mathring{L}\phi-(g^{0\beta}g^{\gamma\kappa}G_{\kappa\beta}^{\nu})\mathring{L}\varphi_{\nu}\big\}\varphi_{\gamma}\\
=&-\{(\mathcal{FG})_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}({\mathring{L}})+2(\mathcal{FG})_{\mathring{L}\tilde{T}}({\mathring{L}})+(\mathcal{FG})_{\tilde{T}\tilde{T}}({\mathring{L}})\}\mathring{L}\phi-\{(\mathcal{FG})_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}({\mathring{L}})\\
&+(\mathcal{FG})_{\mathring{L}\tilde{T}}({\mathring{L}})\}\tilde{T}\phi+\{(\mathcal{FG})_{A\mathring{L}}({\mathring{L}})+(\mathcal{FG})_{A\tilde{T}}({\mathring{L}})\}\not{d}^{A}\phi,\end{split}

(4.111)

\begin{split}&\mathring{L}_{\alpha}(Tg^{\alpha\lambda})\varphi_{\lambda}=-g^{\alpha\lambda}\mathring{L}^{\beta}(Tg_{\alpha\beta})\varphi_{\lambda}\\
=&(\mathcal{FG})_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}(T)(\mathring{L}\phi+\tilde{T}\phi)+(\mathcal{FG})_{\tilde{T}\mathring{L}}(T)\mathring{L}\phi-(\mathcal{FG})_{A\mathring{L}}(T)\not{d}^{A}\phi\qquad\quad\end{split}

(4.112)

and

\begin{split}g^{\alpha\lambda}\not{d}^{A}x^{i}(\not{d}_{A}g_{\alpha i})\varphi_{\lambda}=&-\not{g}^{AB}(\mathcal{FG})_{\mathring{L}A}({X_{B}})(\mathring{L}\phi+\tilde{T}\phi)\\
&-\not{g}^{AB}(\mathcal{FG})_{\tilde{T}A}({X_{B}})\mathring{L}\phi+\not{g}^{AC}(\mathcal{FG})_{AB}({X_{C}})\not{d}^{B}\phi.\end{split}

(4.113)

Substituting (
4.111
)-(
4.113
) into (
4.110
) yields

\begin{split}&(\partial_{\alpha}g^{\alpha\lambda})\varphi_{\lambda}\\
=&-G_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}^{\gamma}(\tilde{T}\varphi_{\gamma})\mathring{L}\phi-G_{\tilde{T}\mathring{L}}^{\gamma}(\tilde{T}\varphi_{\gamma})\mathring{L}\phi+G^{\gamma}_{A\mathring{L}}(\tilde{T}\varphi_{\gamma})\not{d}^{A}\phi\\
&-F_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}(\tilde{T}\phi)^{2}-G_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}^{\gamma}(\tilde{T}\varphi_{\gamma})\tilde{T}\phi+f(\phi,\varphi,\not{d}x^{i},\mathring{L}^{i})\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathring{L}\phi\\
\not{d}\phi\\
\tilde{T}\phi\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathring{L}\phi\\
\not{d}\phi\\
\mathring{L}\varphi\\
\not{d}\varphi\\
\end{array}\right).\end{split}

(4.114)

(b) Similarly, applying the equality (
4.106
) to
g^{\alpha\beta}
and
g^{\kappa\lambda}
yields

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{2}\big(g^{\alpha\beta}F_{\alpha\beta}\big)\big(g^{\kappa\lambda}\varphi_{\kappa}\varphi_{\lambda}\big)+\frac{1}{2}\big(g^{\alpha\beta}G_{\alpha\beta}^{\gamma}\big)\big(g^{\kappa\lambda}\varphi_{\lambda}\partial_{\kappa}\varphi_{\gamma}\big)\\
=&\frac{1}{2}G_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}^{\gamma}(\tilde{T}\varphi_{\gamma})\mathring{L}\phi+G_{\mathring{L}\tilde{T}}^{\gamma}(\tilde{T}\varphi_{\gamma})\mathring{L}\phi-\frac{1}{2}\not{g}^{AB}G_{AB}^{\gamma}(\tilde{T}\varphi_{\gamma})\mathring{L}\phi\\
&+f(\phi,\varphi,\not{d}x^{i},\mathring{L}^{i})\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathring{L}\phi\\
\not{d}\phi\\
\tilde{T}\phi\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathring{L}\phi\\
\not{d}\phi\\
\mathring{L}\varphi\\
\not{d}\varphi\\
\end{array}\right).\end{split}

(4.115)

Thus, by putting (
4.114
)-(
4.115
) into (
4.109
), one can get

\begin{split}&\mu\Box_{g}\phi\\
=&-\frac{1}{2}G_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}^{\gamma}(T\varphi_{\gamma})\mathring{L}\phi+G_{\mathring{L}A}^{\gamma}(T\varphi_{\gamma})\not{d}^{A}\phi-\frac{1}{2}\not{g}^{AB}G_{AB}^{\gamma}(T\varphi_{\gamma})\mathring{L}\phi-F_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}(T\phi)\tilde{T}\phi\\
&-G_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}^{\gamma}(T\varphi_{\gamma})\tilde{T}\phi+f(\phi,\varphi,\not{d}x^{i},\mathring{L}^{i})\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mu\mathring{L}\phi\\
\mu\not{d}\phi\\
T\phi\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathring{L}\phi\\
\not{d}\phi\\
\mathring{L}\varphi\\
\not{d}\varphi\\
\end{array}\right).\end{split}

(4.116)

As explained in Remark
4.3
,
G_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}^{\gamma}(T\varphi_{\gamma})

will admit higher order smallness and faster time-decay rate than the ones for
T\varphi_{\gamma}

because of the null condition (
1.16
). Thus, the term
-G_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}^{\gamma}(T\varphi_{\gamma})\tilde{T}\phi

in the right hand side of (
4.116
) does not give rise to new difficulties for the proof of global existence
in
A_{2\delta}
.

The following two Lemmas are needed to deal with commutators, which can be found from Lemmas 4.10, 8.9 and 8.11 in
[
39
]
.

Lemma 4.7
.

In the frame
\{\mathring{L},T,R_{ij}\}
, the following identities for the commutators hold:

\begin{split}[\mathring{L},R_{ij}]&={{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(R_{ij})}\not{\pi}_{\mathring{L}}}^{A}X_{A},\\
[\mathring{L},T]&={{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(T)}\not{\pi}_{\mathring{L}}}^{A}X_{A},\\
[T,R_{ij}]&={{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(R_{ij})}\not{\pi}_{T}}^{A}X_{A}.\end{split}

(4.117)

Lemma 4.8
.

For any vector field
Z\in\{\mathring{L},T,R_{ij}\}
,

(a) if
f
is a smooth function, then

\displaystyle\big([\not{\nabla}^{2},\not{\mathcal{L}}_{Z}]f\big)_{AB}

\displaystyle=\big(\check{\not{\nabla}}_{A}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}_{B}}}^{(Z)}\not{\pi}_{B}^{C}\big)\not{d}_{C}f,

\displaystyle[\not{\triangle},Z]f

\displaystyle={\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}^{(Z)}\not{\pi}^{AB}\not{\nabla}_{AB}^{2}f+(\check{\not{\nabla}}_{A}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}^{(Z)}\not{\pi}^{AB})\not{d}_{B}f;

(b) if
\xi
is a one-form on
S_{s,u}
, then

([\not{\nabla}_{A},\not{\mathcal{L}}_{Z}]\xi)_{B}=(\check{\not{\nabla}}_{A}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}_{B}}}^{(Z)}\not{\pi}_{B}^{C})\xi_{C};

(c) if
\xi
is a
(0,2)
-type symmetric tensor on
S_{s,u}
, then

\displaystyle([\not{\nabla}_{A},\not{\mathcal{L}}_{Z}]\xi)_{BC}

\displaystyle=(\check{\not{\nabla}}_{A}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}_{B}}}^{(Z)}\not{\pi}_{B}^{D})\xi_{CD}+(\check{\not{\nabla}}_{A}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}_{C}}}^{(Z)}\not{\pi}_{C}^{D})\xi_{BD};

(d) if
\xi
is a
(0,3)
-type tensor on
S_{s,u}
, then

([\not{\nabla}_{A},\not{\mathcal{L}}_{Z}]\xi)_{BCD}=(\check{\not{\nabla}}_{A}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}_{B}}}^{(Z)}\not{\pi}_{B}^{E})\xi_{ECD}+(\check{\not{\nabla}}_{A}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}_{C}}}^{(Z)}\not{\pi}_{C}^{E})\xi_{BED}+(\check{\not{\nabla}}_{A}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}_{D}}}^{(Z)}\not{\pi}_{D}^{E})\xi_{BCE},

where

\check{\not{\nabla}}_{A}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}_{BC}}}^{(Z)}\not{\pi}_{BC}:=\frac{1}{2}\not{\nabla}_{A}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}_{BC}}}^{(Z)}\not{\pi}_{BC}+\frac{1}{2}\not{\nabla}_{B}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}_{AC}}}^{(Z)}\not{\pi}_{AC}-\frac{1}{2}\not{\nabla}_{C}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}_{AB}}}^{(Z)}\not{\pi}_{AB}.

5.
Bootstrap assumptions on
(\phi,\partial\phi)
near
C_{0}
and some related estimates

To show the global existence of the solution
\phi
to (
1.10
) near
C_{0}
,
we will a bootstrap argument. To this end, the following bootstrap assumptions
are made in
D^{s,u}
:

\begin{split}&\delta^{l}\|\mathring{L}Z^{\alpha}\varphi_{\gamma}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{s}^{u})}\leq M\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-5/2},\\
&\delta^{l}\|\not{d}Z^{\alpha}\varphi_{\gamma}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{s}^{u})}\leq M\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-5/2},\\
&\delta^{l}\|\mathring{\underline{L}}Z^{\alpha}\varphi_{\gamma}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{s}^{u})}\leq M\delta^{-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-3/2},\\
&\|\not{\nabla}^{2}\varphi_{\gamma}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{s}^{u})}\leq M\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-7/2},\\
&\|\varphi_{\gamma}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{s}^{u})}\leq M\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-3/2},\\
&\delta^{l}\|Z^{\beta}\phi\|_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{s}^{u})}\leq M\delta^{2-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-3/2},\\
&\|\not{d}\phi\|_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{s}^{u})}\leq M\delta^{2-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-5/2},\\
&\|\not{\nabla}^{2}\phi\|_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{s}^{u})}\leq M\delta^{2-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-7/2},\end{split}

(
\star
)

where
|\alpha|\leq N
,
|\beta|\leq N+1
,
N
is a fixed large positive integer,
M
is some positive number to
be suitably chosen
(at least double bounds of the corresponding quantities on time
t_{0}
),

Z\in\{\varrho\mathring{L},T,R_{ij}\}
, and
l
is the number of
T
in
Z^{\alpha}
or
Z^{\beta}
.

We now give a rough estimate of
\mu
under the assumptions (
\star
). Note that

1=g_{ij}\tilde{T}^{i}\tilde{T}^{j}=\big(1+O(M\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-3/2})\big)\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{4}|\tilde{T}^{i}|^{2}

by (
4.74
). This means

|\tilde{T}^{i}|,|\mathring{L}^{i}|\leq 1+O(M\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-3/2})

(5.118)

due to
\mathring{L}^{i}=-g^{0i}-\tilde{T}^{i}
.
Moreover, by
|\not{d}x^{i}|^{2}=\not{g}^{ab}\not{d}_{a}x^{i}\not{d}_{b}x^{i}=g^{ii}+(g^{0i})^{2}-(\tilde{T}^{i})^{2}
,
(\star)
and (
5.118
),
it holds that

|\not{d}x^{i}|\lesssim 1.

(5.119)

Then
|\mathring{L}(\varrho\check{L}^{i})|\lesssim M\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-3/2}
holds by (
4.93
) and
(\star)
, which further gives

|\check{L}^{i}|+|\check{T}^{i}|\lesssim M\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-1}

(5.120)

through integrating
\mathring{L}(\varrho\check{L}^{i})
along integral curves of
\mathring{L}
and
using
\check{T}^{i}=-g^{0i}-\check{L}^{i}
.
In addition, by
\displaystyle g_{ij}(\check{T}^{i}-\frac{x^{i}}{\varrho})(\check{T}^{j}-\frac{x^{j}}{\varrho})=1
, one has

(g_{ij}\omega^{i}\omega^{j})\frac{r^{2}}{\varrho^{2}}-(2g_{ij}\check{T}^{i}\omega^{j})\frac{r}{\varrho}+g_{ij}\check{T}^{i}\check{T}^{j}-1=0.

Thus

\check{\varrho}:=\frac{r}{\varrho}-1=\frac{1-g_{ij}\omega^{i}\omega^{j}-g_{ij}\check{T}^{i}\check{T}^{j}+2g_{ij}\check{T}^{i}\omega^{j}}{\sqrt{g_{ij}\omega^{i}\omega^{j}-(g_{ij}\omega^{i}\omega^{j})(g_{ab}\check{T}^{a}\check{T}^{b})+(g_{ij}\check{T}^{i}\omega^{j})^{2}}+g_{ij}\omega^{i}\omega^{j}-g_{ij}\check{T}^{i}\omega^{j}}.

(5.121)

It follows from (
5.120
) and (
5.121
) that

|\check{\varrho}|\lesssim M\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-1},

(5.122)

and hence by (
4.83
), one has

|\upsilon_{ij}|\lesssim\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}M.

(5.123)

In order to estimate
\mu
, we pay special attention to the last term in (
4.86
) since it contains the factor
T\varphi_{\gamma}

that seems to have the worse order of smallness and the slower time-decay rate due to
(\star)
.
By (
4.107
) and (
4.103
), one can arrive at

\begin{split}G_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}^{\gamma}T\varphi_{\gamma}=&G_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}^{0}T^{i}(\mathring{L}\varphi_{i})-(\partial_{\varphi_{\gamma}}g)_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}\mathring{L}_{\gamma}\tilde{T}^{i}T\varphi_{i}+g_{\gamma i}G_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}^{\gamma}(\not{d}^{A}x^{i})T^{j}\not{d}_{A}\varphi_{j}\\
=&G_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}^{0}T^{i}(\mathring{L}\varphi_{i})+\underline{(\partial_{\varphi_{\gamma}}g^{\alpha\beta})\mathring{L}_{\alpha}\mathring{L}_{\beta}\mathring{L}_{\gamma}}\tilde{T}^{i}T\varphi_{i}+g_{\gamma i}G_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}^{\gamma}(\not{d}^{A}x^{i})T^{j}\not{d}_{A}\varphi_{j}.\end{split}

(5.124)

Due to the first null condition (
1.16
),
the underline factor in (
5.124
) can be written as

\begin{split}&(\partial_{\varphi_{\gamma}}g^{\alpha\beta})\mathring{L}_{\alpha}\mathring{L}_{\beta}\mathring{L}_{\gamma}\\
=&\{(\partial_{\varphi_{\gamma}}g^{\alpha\beta})-\tilde{g}^{\alpha\beta,\gamma}\}\mathring{L}_{\alpha}\mathring{L}_{\beta}\mathring{L}_{\gamma}+\tilde{g}^{\alpha\beta,\gamma}(g_{\kappa\alpha}g_{\lambda\beta}g_{\nu\gamma}-m_{\kappa\alpha}m_{\lambda\beta}m_{\nu\gamma})\mathring{L}^{\kappa}\mathring{L}^{\lambda}\mathring{L}^{\nu}\\
&+\tilde{g}^{\alpha\beta,\gamma}m_{\kappa\alpha}m_{\lambda\beta}m_{\nu\gamma}\big\{(\check{L}^{\kappa}+\frac{\tilde{x}^{\kappa}}{\varrho})(\check{L}^{\lambda}+\frac{\tilde{x}^{\lambda}}{\varrho})(\check{L}^{\nu}+\frac{\tilde{x}^{\nu}}{\varrho})-\frac{\tilde{x}^{\kappa}}{\varrho}\frac{\tilde{x}^{\lambda}}{\varrho}\frac{\tilde{x}^{\nu}}{\varrho}\big\}\\
&+\tilde{g}^{\alpha\beta,\gamma}\big\{(\omega_{\alpha}+m_{\alpha i}\frac{\check{\varrho}x^{i}}{(\check{\varrho}+1)\varrho})(\omega_{\beta}+m_{\beta j}\frac{\check{\varrho}x^{j}}{(\check{\varrho}+1)\varrho})(\omega_{\gamma}+m_{\gamma k}\frac{\check{\varrho}x^{k}}{(\check{\varrho}+1)\varrho})-\omega_{\alpha}\omega_{\beta}\omega_{\gamma}\big\},\end{split}

(5.125)

where
(\tilde{x}^{0},\tilde{x}^{1},\tilde{x}^{2},\tilde{x}^{3},\tilde{x}^{4})=(1,x^{1},x^{2},x^{3},x^{4})

and
(\omega_{0},\omega_{1},\omega_{2},\omega_{3},\omega_{4})=(-1,\omega^{1},\omega^{2},\omega^{3},\omega^{4})
. Therefore,

|(\partial_{\varphi_{\gamma}}g^{\alpha\beta})\mathring{L}_{\alpha}\mathring{L}_{\beta}\mathring{L}_{\gamma}|\lesssim M\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-1}

holds by (
5.118
)-(
5.120
), (
5.122
) and
(\star)
.
Subsequently, it follows from (
5.124
) that

|G_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}^{\gamma}T\varphi_{\gamma}|\lesssim\mu M\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-5/2}+M^{2}\delta^{1-2\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-5/2}.

(5.126)

This, together with
(\star)
and (
4.86
), implies that

|\mathring{L}\mu|\lesssim M\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-5/2}\mu+M^{2}\delta^{1-2\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-3/2}
. When
\delta>0
is small, by
integrating
\mathring{L}\mu
along integral curves of
\mathring{L}
and noting
\displaystyle\mu=\frac{1}{\sqrt{(g^{0i}\omega_{i})^{2}+g^{ij}\omega_{i}\omega_{j}}}=1+O(\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}})
on
\Sigma_{t_{0}}
,
one can get

\mu=1+O(M^{2}\delta^{1-2\varepsilon_{0}}).

(5.127)

To improve the assumptions (
\star
) and close the bootstrap arguments, we rewrite the equation (
4.108
) in the new frame

\{\mathring{\underline{L}},\mathring{L},X_{1},X_{2},X_{3}\}
as

\mathring{L}\mathring{\underline{L}}\varphi_{\gamma}+\frac{3}{2\varrho}\mathring{\underline{L}}\varphi_{\gamma}=\mu\not{\triangle}\varphi_{\gamma}+H_{\gamma},

(5.128)

here one has used the fact
\mu\Box_{g}\varphi_{\gamma}=-\mathscr{D}_{\mathring{L}\mathring{\underline{L}}}^{2}\varphi_{\gamma}+\mu\not{g}^{AB}\mathscr{D}_{AB}^{2}\varphi_{\gamma}=-\mathring{L}\mathring{\underline{L}}\varphi_{\gamma}-2\mu\zeta^{A}(\not{d}_{A}\varphi_{\gamma})+\mu\not{\triangle}\varphi_{\gamma}-\mu(\textrm{tr}\sigma+\textrm{tr}\chi)\mathring{L}\varphi_{\gamma}-\textrm{tr}\chi T\varphi_{\gamma}

due to (
4.100
) and (
4.101
). In addition, by (
4.97
)-(
4.98
),

\begin{split}H_{\gamma}=&-\frac{1}{2}\textrm{tr}\check{\chi}(\mathring{\underline{L}}\varphi_{\gamma}-\mu\mathring{L}\varphi_{\gamma})+\frac{3}{2\varrho}\mu\mathring{L}\varphi_{\gamma}\\
&+f_{1}(\phi,\varphi,\mathring{L}^{i})\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mu\mathring{L}\phi\\
\mu\mathring{L}\varphi\\
\mu(\not{d}_{A}x^{j})\not{d}^{A}\varphi\\
\mathring{\underline{L}}\phi\\
\mathring{\underline{L}}\varphi\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathring{L}\phi\\
\mathring{L}\varphi\\
(\not{d}_{B}x^{k})\not{d}^{B}\phi\\
(\not{d}_{B}x^{k})\not{d}^{B}\varphi\\
\end{array}\right)+f_{2}(\phi,\varphi,\mathring{L}^{i})\not{d}^{A}\varphi\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mu\not{d}_{A}\phi\\
\mu\not{d}_{A}\varphi\end{array}\right).\end{split}

(5.129)

It should be observed here that in
H_{\gamma}
, wherever there is a term containing a factor of the form
\mathring{\underline{L}}\varphi
(or
\mathring{\underline{L}}\phi
) which admits the “bad” order of smallness or slow time decay rate, then the term must contain another factor with the “good” order of smallness and fast time-decay rate, e.g.

\textrm{tr}\check{\chi},\mathring{L}\phi,\mathring{L}\varphi
and so on. Consequently,
H_{\gamma}
may
be expected to have some desired “good” properties.

Similarly, the left side of (
4.116
) can be replaced by
\mu\Box_{g}\phi=-\mathring{L}\mathring{\underline{L}}\phi-2\mu\zeta^{A}(\not{d}_{A}\phi)+\mu\not{\triangle}\phi-\mu(\textrm{tr}\sigma+\textrm{tr}\chi)\mathring{L}\phi-\textrm{tr}\chi T\phi
, and hence,

\mathring{L}\mathring{\underline{L}}\phi+\frac{3}{2\varrho}\mathring{\underline{L}}\phi=\mathring{H}+\mu\not{\triangle}\phi

(5.130)

with

\begin{split}\mathring{H}=&\frac{3}{2\varrho}\mu\mathring{L}\phi-\frac{1}{2}\text{tr}\check{\chi}(\underline{\mathring{L}}\phi-\mu\mathring{L}\phi)+\frac{1}{2}G_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}^{\gamma}(T\varphi_{\gamma})\mathring{L}\phi+F_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}(\tilde{T}\phi)T\phi\\
&+G_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}^{\gamma}(T\varphi_{\gamma})\tilde{T}\phi+f(\phi,\varphi,\not{d}x^{i},\mathring{L}^{i})\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mu\mathring{L}\phi\\
\mu\not{d}\phi\\
T\phi\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathring{L}\phi\\
\not{d}\phi\\
\mathring{L}\varphi\\
\not{d}\varphi\\
\end{array}\right).\end{split}

(5.131)

Unless stated otherwise, in what follows until Section
10
, the pointwise estimates for the related quantities
are all made inside domain
D^{s,u}
.

It follows from (
5.128
) that
\mathring{\underline{L}}\varphi_{\gamma}
can be estimated by integrating (
5.128
)
along integral curves of
\mathring{L}
. To this end, one should estimate
\check{\chi}
and other terms in

H_{\gamma}
.

To treat
\check{\chi}
, we now derive a structure equation for
\chi
by utilizing the Riemann curvature of
metric
g
.
As the Definition 11.1 in
[
39
]
, the Riemann curvature tensor
\mathscr{R}
of
g
can be defined as follows:

\mathscr{R}_{WXYZ}:=-g(\mathscr{D}_{WX}^{2}Y-\mathscr{D}_{XW}^{2}Y,Z),

(5.132)

where
W
,
X
,
Y
and
Z
are vector fields and
\mathscr{D}_{WX}^{2}Y:=W^{\alpha}X^{\beta}\mathscr{D}_{\alpha}\mathscr{D}_{\beta}Y
.

Lemma 5.1
.

In the frame
\{\mathring{L},T,X_{1},X_{2},X_{3}\}
,

\mathscr{R}_{TA\mathring{L}B}
and
\mathscr{R}_{\mathring{L}A\mathring{L}B}
are given as follows:

\begin{split}\mathscr{R}_{TA\mathring{L}B}=&\frac{1}{2}\big\{\mu F_{B\tilde{T}}\not{d}_{A}\mathring{L}\phi+\mu G_{B\tilde{T}}^{\gamma}\not{d}_{A}\mathring{L}\varphi_{\gamma}-\mu F_{\mathring{L}\tilde{T}}\not{\nabla}_{AB}^{2}\phi-\mu G_{\mathring{L}\tilde{T}}^{\gamma}\not{\nabla}_{AB}^{2}\varphi_{\gamma}\\
&\quad-F_{AB}\mathring{L}T\phi-G_{AB}^{\gamma}\mathring{L}T\varphi_{\gamma}+F_{A\mathring{L}}\not{d}_{B}T\phi+G_{A\mathring{L}}^{\gamma}\not{d}_{B}T\varphi_{\gamma}\big\}\\
&+\frac{1}{4}\mu^{-1}\big\{(\mathcal{FG})_{A\mathring{L}}(T)\cdot(\mathcal{FG})_{B\mathring{L}}(T)-2(\mathcal{FG})_{A\mathring{L}}(T)(\not{d}_{B}\mu)\big\}\\
&+\frac{1}{2}\big\{\mu(\mathcal{FG})_{A\mathring{L}}(X_{C}){\chi_{B}}^{C}-\mu(\mathcal{FG})_{B\tilde{T}}(X_{C}){\chi_{A}}^{C}+(\mathcal{FG})_{\mathring{L}\tilde{T}}(T)\chi_{AB}\big\}\\
&+f(\phi,\varphi,\not{d}x^{i},\mathring{L}^{i})\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mu\mathring{L}\phi\\
\mu\not{d}\phi\\
T\phi\\
\mu\mathring{L}\varphi\\
\mu\not{d}\varphi\\
T{\varphi}\\
\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathring{L}\phi\\
\not{d}\phi\\
\mathring{L}\varphi\\
\not{d}\varphi\\
\end{array}\right),\end{split}

(5.133)

and

\begin{split}\mathscr{R}_{\mathring{L}A\mathring{L}B}=&\frac{1}{2}\big\{F_{B\mathring{L}}\not{d}_{A}\mathring{L}\phi+G_{B\mathring{L}}^{\gamma}(\not{d}_{A}\mathring{L}\varphi_{\gamma})-F_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}\not{\nabla}^{2}_{AB}\phi-G_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}^{\gamma}\not{\nabla}^{2}_{AB}\varphi_{\gamma}-F_{AB}\mathring{L}^{2}\phi\\
&-G_{AB}^{\gamma}\mathring{L}^{2}\varphi_{\gamma}+F_{A\mathring{L}}\not{d}_{B}\mathring{L}\phi+G_{A\mathring{L}}^{\gamma}\not{d}_{B}\mathring{L}\varphi_{\gamma}\}-\frac{1}{2}(\mathcal{FG})_{B\mathring{L}}(X_{C}){\chi_{A}}^{C}\\
&-\frac{1}{2}(\mathcal{FG})_{A\mathring{L}}(X_{C}){\chi_{B}}^{C}+\frac{1}{2}\mu^{-1}(\mathcal{FG})_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}(T)\chi_{AB}\\
&+f(\phi,\varphi,\not{d}x^{i},\mathring{L}^{i})\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathring{L}\phi\\
\not{d}\phi\\
\mathring{L}\varphi\\
\not{d}\varphi\\
\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathring{L}\phi\\
\not{d}\phi\\
\mathring{L}\varphi\\
\not{d}\varphi\\
\end{array}\right),\end{split}

(5.134)

where
f(\phi,\varphi,\not{d}x^{i},\mathring{L}^{i})
represents a generic smooth function of its arguments and similar convention as (
4.108
) has been used.

Proof.

Note that
\mathscr{R}
is a
(0,4)
-type tensor field. Taking
W=\partial_{\kappa}
,
X=\partial_{\lambda}
,
Y=\partial_{\alpha}
and
Z=\partial_{\beta}
in (
5.132
), and
applying
\mathscr{D}_{\alpha}\partial_{\beta}=\Gamma^{\nu}_{\alpha\gamma\beta}\partial_{\nu}
, one can get the components of
\mathscr{R}
as

\mathscr{R}_{\kappa\lambda\alpha\beta}=\partial_{\lambda}\Gamma_{\alpha\beta\kappa}-\partial_{\kappa}\Gamma_{\lambda\beta\alpha}-g^{\nu\gamma}\Gamma_{\alpha\gamma\kappa}\Gamma_{\lambda\nu\beta}+g^{\nu\gamma}\Gamma_{\lambda\gamma\alpha}\Gamma_{\kappa\nu\beta}

with the Christoffel symbol
\Gamma_{\alpha\beta\kappa}:=g_{\beta\gamma}\Gamma_{\alpha\kappa}^{\gamma}
.
Therefore,

\begin{split}\mathscr{R}_{\kappa\lambda\alpha\beta}=&\frac{1}{2}\big\{F_{\beta\kappa}\mathscr{D}_{\lambda\alpha}^{2}\phi+G_{\beta\kappa}^{\nu}\mathscr{D}_{\lambda\alpha}^{2}\varphi_{\nu}-F_{\alpha\kappa}\mathscr{D}_{\lambda\beta}^{2}\phi-G_{\alpha\kappa}^{\nu}\mathscr{D}_{\lambda\beta}^{2}\varphi_{\nu}\\
&\quad-F_{\beta\lambda}\mathscr{D}_{\kappa\alpha}^{2}\phi-G_{\beta\lambda}^{\nu}\mathscr{D}_{\kappa\alpha}^{2}\varphi_{\nu}+F_{\alpha\lambda}\mathscr{D}_{\kappa\beta}^{2}\phi+G_{\alpha\lambda}^{\nu}\mathscr{D}_{\kappa\beta}^{2}\varphi_{\nu}\big\}\\
&-\frac{1}{4}g^{\tau\gamma}(F_{\lambda\alpha}\partial_{\gamma}\phi+G_{\lambda\alpha}^{\sigma}\partial_{\gamma}\varphi_{\sigma})(F_{\kappa\beta}\partial_{\tau}\phi+G_{\beta\kappa}^{\nu}\partial_{\tau}\varphi_{\nu})\\
&+\frac{1}{4}g^{\tau\gamma}(F_{\lambda\beta}\partial_{\gamma}\phi+G_{\lambda\beta}^{\sigma}\partial_{\gamma}\varphi_{\sigma})(F_{\alpha\kappa}\partial_{\tau}\phi+G_{\alpha\kappa}^{\nu}\partial_{\tau}\varphi_{\nu})\\
&-\frac{1}{4}g^{\tau\gamma}\big(F_{\gamma(\kappa}\partial_{\alpha)}\phi+G_{\gamma(\kappa}^{\sigma}\partial_{\alpha)}\varphi_{\sigma}\big)\big(F_{\tau(\lambda}\partial_{\beta)}\phi+G_{\tau(\lambda}^{\nu}\partial_{\beta)}\varphi_{\nu}\big)\\
&+\frac{1}{4}g^{\tau\gamma}\big(F_{\gamma(\lambda}\partial_{\alpha)}\phi+G_{\gamma(\lambda}^{\sigma}\partial_{\alpha)}\varphi_{\sigma}\big)\big(F_{\tau(\kappa}\partial_{\beta)}\phi+G_{\tau(\kappa}^{\nu}\partial_{\beta)}\varphi_{\nu}\big)\\
&+\frac{1}{2}\big\{(\partial_{\phi}F_{\beta\kappa})(\partial_{\lambda}\phi)(\partial_{\alpha}\phi)+(\partial_{\varphi_{\sigma}}F_{\beta\kappa})(\partial_{\lambda}\varphi_{\sigma})(\partial_{\alpha}\phi)+(\partial_{\phi}G_{\beta\kappa}^{\sigma})(\partial_{\lambda}\phi)(\partial_{\alpha}\varphi_{\sigma})\\
&+(\partial_{\varphi_{\sigma}}G_{\beta\kappa}^{\gamma})(\partial_{\lambda}\varphi_{\sigma})(\partial_{\alpha}\varphi_{\gamma})-(\partial_{\phi}F_{\alpha\kappa})(\partial_{\lambda}\phi)(\partial_{\beta}\phi)-(\partial_{\varphi_{\sigma}}F_{\alpha\kappa})(\partial_{\lambda}\varphi_{\sigma})(\partial_{\beta}\phi)\\
&-(\partial_{\phi}G_{\alpha\kappa}^{\gamma})(\partial_{\lambda}\phi)(\partial_{\beta}\varphi_{\gamma})-(\partial_{\varphi_{\sigma}}G_{\alpha\kappa}^{\gamma})(\partial_{\lambda}\varphi_{\sigma})(\partial_{\beta}\varphi_{\gamma})-(\partial_{\phi}F_{\lambda\beta})(\partial_{\kappa}\phi)(\partial_{\alpha}\phi)\\
&-(\partial_{\varphi_{\sigma}}F_{\lambda\beta})(\partial_{\kappa}\varphi_{\sigma})(\partial_{\alpha}\phi)-(\partial_{\phi}G_{\lambda\beta}^{\gamma})(\partial_{\kappa}\phi)(\partial_{\alpha}\varphi_{\gamma})-(\partial_{\varphi_{\sigma}}G_{\lambda\beta}^{\gamma})(\partial_{\kappa}\varphi_{\sigma})(\partial_{\alpha}\varphi_{\gamma})\\
&+(\partial_{\phi}F_{\lambda\alpha})(\partial_{\kappa}\phi)(\partial_{\beta}\phi)+(\partial_{\varphi_{\sigma}}F_{\lambda\alpha})(\partial_{\kappa}\varphi_{\sigma})(\partial_{\beta}\phi)+(\partial_{\phi}G_{\lambda\alpha}^{\gamma})(\partial_{\kappa}\phi)(\partial_{\beta}\varphi_{\gamma})\\
&+(\partial_{\varphi_{\sigma}}G_{\lambda\alpha}^{\gamma})(\partial_{\kappa}\varphi_{\sigma})(\partial_{\beta}\varphi_{\gamma})\big\},\end{split}

where
F_{\kappa(\lambda}\partial_{\alpha)}\phi:=F_{\kappa\lambda}\partial_{\alpha}\phi+F_{\kappa\alpha}\partial_{\lambda}\phi
and
G_{\kappa(\lambda}^{\nu}\partial_{\alpha)}\varphi_{\nu}:=G_{\kappa\lambda}^{\nu}\partial_{\alpha}\varphi_{\nu}+G_{\kappa\alpha}^{\nu}\partial_{\lambda}\varphi_{\nu}
.
In addition, by (
4.106
), one has that for any generic smooth functions
\psi_{i}

(i=1,2)
,

g^{\tau\gamma}(\partial_{\tau}\psi_{1})(\partial_{\gamma}\psi_{2})=\not{g}^{AB}(\not{d}_{A}\psi_{1})(\not{d}_{B}\psi_{2})-(\mathring{L}\psi_{1})(\mathring{L}\psi_{2})-\mu^{-1}(T\psi_{1})(\mathring{L}\psi_{2})-\mu^{-1}(T\psi_{2})(\mathring{L}\psi_{1}).

Then contracting
\mathscr{R}_{\kappa\lambda\alpha\beta}
with
T^{\kappa}(\not{d}_{A}x^{\lambda})\mathring{L}^{\alpha}(\not{d}_{B}x^{\beta})
yields

\begin{split}\mathscr{R}_{TA\mathring{L}B}=&\frac{1}{2}\big(\mu F_{B\tilde{T}}\mathscr{D}_{A\mathring{L}}^{2}\phi+\mu G_{B\tilde{T}}^{\nu}\mathscr{D}_{A\mathring{L}}^{2}\varphi_{\nu}-\mu F_{\mathring{L}\tilde{T}}\mathscr{D}_{AB}^{2}\phi-\mu G_{\mathring{L}\tilde{T}}^{\nu}\mathscr{D}_{AB}^{2}\varphi_{\nu}\\
&\quad-F_{AB}\mathscr{D}^{2}_{\mathring{L}T}\phi-G_{AB}^{\nu}\mathscr{D}^{2}_{\mathring{L}T}\varphi_{\nu}+F_{A\mathring{L}}\mathscr{D}_{BT}^{2}\phi+G_{A\mathring{L}}^{\nu}\mathscr{D}_{BT}^{2}\varphi_{\nu}\big)\\
\end{split}

\begin{split}&+f(\phi,\varphi,\not{d}x^{i},\mathring{L}^{i})\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mu\mathring{L}\phi\\
\mu\not{d}\phi\\
T\phi\\
\mu\mathring{L}\varphi\\
\mu\not{d}\varphi\\
T{\varphi}\\
\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathring{L}\phi\\
\not{d}\phi\\
\mathring{L}\varphi\\
\not{d}\varphi\\
\end{array}\right).\end{split}

(5.135)

In addition, it follows from (
4.98
), (
4.97
) and Lemma
4.5
that for any smooth function
\psi
,

\begin{split}\mathscr{D}_{A\mathring{L}}^{2}\psi=&\not{d}_{A}\mathring{L}\psi-(\mathscr{D}_{A}\mathring{L})\psi\\
=&\not{d}_{A}\mathring{L}\psi-\chi_{AB}\not{d}^{B}\psi+f(\phi,\varphi,\not{d}x^{i},\mathring{L}^{i})\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathring{L}\phi\\
\not{d}\phi\\
\tilde{T}\phi\\
\mathring{L}\varphi\\
\not{d}\varphi\\
\tilde{T}{\varphi}\\
\end{array}\right)\mathring{L}\psi.\end{split}

(5.136)

Similarly,

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}_{BT}^{2}\psi=\not{d}_{B}T\psi-(\mu^{-1}T\psi)\not{d}_{B}\mu+(\mu\not{d}^{D}\psi)\chi_{BD}+\frac{1}{2}F_{B\mathring{L}}\tilde{T}\phi(T\psi)+\frac{1}{2}G_{B\mathring{L}}^{\gamma}\tilde{T}\varphi_{\gamma}(T\psi)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+f_{1}(\phi,\varphi,\not{d}x^{i},\mathring{L}^{i})\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mu\mathring{L}\phi\\
\mu\not{d}\phi\\
T\phi\\
\mu\mathring{L}\varphi\\
\mu\not{d}\varphi\\
T{\varphi}\\
\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathring{L}\psi\\
\not{d}\psi\\
\end{array}\right)+f_{2}(\phi,\varphi,\not{d}x^{i},\mathring{L}^{i})\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathring{L}\phi\\
\not{d}\phi\\
\mathring{L}\varphi\\
\not{d}\varphi\\
\end{array}\right)T\psi,

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}_{\mathring{L}T}^{2}\psi=\mathring{L}T\psi+f(\phi,\varphi,\not{d}x^{i},\mathring{L}^{i})\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mu\mathring{L}\phi\\
\mu\not{d}\phi\\
T\phi\\
\mu\mathring{L}\varphi\\
\mu\not{d}\varphi\\
T{\varphi}\\
\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathring{L}\psi\\
\not{d}\psi\\
\end{array}\right),

\displaystyle\mu\mathscr{D}_{AB}^{2}\psi=\mu\not{\nabla}_{AB}^{2}\psi-\chi_{AB}(T\psi)+f(\phi,\varphi,\not{d}x^{i},\mathring{L}^{i})\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mu\mathring{L}\phi\\
\mu\not{d}\phi\\
T\phi\\
\mu\mathring{L}\varphi\\
\mu\not{d}\varphi\\
T{\varphi}\\
\end{array}\right)\mathring{L}\psi.

Taking
\psi=\phi
or
\varphi_{\gamma}
in (
5.136
)-(
5
), and then substituting these resulting equations into (
5.135
), one gets (
5.133
) immediately.

By an analogous argument for (
5.133
), one can show (
5.134
). Here details are omitted.
∎

In Lemma
5.1
, the expressions of
\mathscr{R}_{TA\mathring{L}B}
and
\mathscr{R}_{\mathring{L}A\mathring{L}B}

are obtained through contracting
\mathscr{R}_{\kappa\lambda\alpha\beta}
with respect to the corresponding vectorfields.
If the definition (
5.132
) is used directly, e.g.
\mathscr{R}_{\mathring{L}A\mathring{L}B}=g\big(\mathscr{D}_{A}(\mathscr{D}_{\mathring{L}}\mathring{L})-\mathscr{D}_{\mathring{L}}(\mathscr{D}_{A}\mathring{L}),X_{B}\big)
,
then one can find the following structure equations for
\chi_{AB}
and
{\check{\chi}}_{AB}
with
the help of Lemmas
5.1
.

Lemma 5.2
.

For the second fundamental form
\chi
and its “error” form
\check{\chi}
defined in (
4.77
) and (
4.80
)
respectively, the following structure equations hold:

\begin{split}\mathring{L}\chi_{AB}=&-\frac{1}{2}\big\{F_{B\mathring{L}}\not{d}_{A}\mathring{L}\phi+G_{B\mathring{L}}^{\gamma}\not{d}_{A}\mathring{L}\varphi_{\gamma}-F_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}\not{\nabla}_{AB}^{2}\phi-G_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}^{\gamma}\not{\nabla}_{AB}^{2}\varphi_{\gamma}-F_{AB}\mathring{L}^{2}\phi\\
&\qquad-G_{AB}^{\gamma}\mathring{L}^{2}\varphi_{\gamma}+F_{A\mathring{L}}\not{d}_{B}\mathring{L}\phi+G_{A\mathring{L}}^{\gamma}\not{d}_{B}\mathring{L}\varphi_{\gamma}+(\mathcal{FG})_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}(\mathring{L})\chi_{AB}+2(\mathcal{FG})_{\tilde{T}\mathring{L}}(\mathring{L})\chi_{AB}\\
&\qquad-(\mathcal{FG})_{B\mathring{L}}(X_{C}){\chi_{A}}^{C}-(\mathcal{FG})_{A\mathring{L}}(X_{C}){\chi_{B}}^{C}\big\}\\
&\quad+{\chi_{A}}^{C}\chi_{BC}+f(\phi,\varphi,\not{d}x^{i},\mathring{L}^{i})\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathring{L}\phi\\
\not{d}\phi\\
\mathring{L}\varphi\\
\not{d}\varphi\\
\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathring{L}\phi\\
\not{d}\phi\\
\mathring{L}\varphi\\
\not{d}\varphi\\
\end{array}\right),\end{split}

(5.163)

\begin{split}\not{\mathcal{L}}_{T}\chi_{AB}=&\not{\nabla}_{AB}^{2}\mu-\frac{1}{2}\big\{\mu F_{\tilde{T}\tilde{T}}\not{\nabla}_{AB}^{2}\phi+\mu G_{\tilde{T}\tilde{T}}^{\gamma}\not{\nabla}_{AB}^{2}\varphi_{\gamma}+F_{B\mathring{L}}\not{d}_{A}T\phi+G_{B\mathring{L}}^{\gamma}\not{d}_{A}T\varphi_{\gamma}\\
&+F_{A\mathring{L}}\not{d}_{B}T\phi+G_{A\mathring{L}}^{\gamma}\not{d}_{B}T\varphi_{\gamma}-F_{AB}\mathring{L}T\phi-G_{AB}^{\gamma}\mathring{L}T\varphi_{\gamma}\big\}-\frac{1}{2}\Theta_{B}\not{d}_{A}\mu-\frac{1}{2}\Theta_{A}\not{d}_{B}\mu\\
&+\frac{1}{2}\big\{\mu(\mathcal{FG})_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}(\mathring{L})+2\mu(\mathcal{FG})_{\tilde{T}\mathring{L}}(\mathring{L})-(\mathcal{FG})_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}(T)-(\mathcal{FG})_{\mathring{L}\tilde{T}}(T)\big\}\chi_{AB}\\
&-\mu{\chi_{A}}^{C}\chi_{BC}-\frac{1}{4}{\chi_{B}}^{C}\Upsilon_{CA}-\frac{1}{4}{\chi_{A}}^{C}\Upsilon_{CB}\\
\end{split}

\begin{split}&+f_{1}(\phi,\varphi,\not{d}x^{i},\mathring{L}^{i})\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\not{d}\mathring{L}^{j}\\
\mathring{L}\phi\\
\not{d}\phi\\
\mathring{L}\varphi\\
\not{d}\varphi\\
\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mu\mathring{L}\phi\\
\mu\not{d}\phi\\
T\phi\\
\mu\mathring{L}\varphi\\
\mu\not{d}\varphi\\
T{\varphi}\\
\end{array}\right)+f_{2}(\phi,\varphi,\mathring{L}^{i})\not{\nabla}_{AB}^{2}x^{j}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mu\mathring{L}\phi\\
T\phi\\
\mu\mathring{L}\varphi\\
T{\varphi}\\
\end{array}\right).\end{split}

(5.164)

And hence,

\begin{split}\mathring{L}\check{\chi}_{AB}=&-\frac{1}{2}\big\{F_{B\mathring{L}}\not{d}_{A}\mathring{L}\phi+G_{B\mathring{L}}^{\gamma}\not{d}_{A}\mathring{L}\varphi_{\gamma}-F_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}\not{\nabla}_{AB}^{2}\phi-G_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}^{\gamma}\not{\nabla}_{AB}^{2}\varphi_{\gamma}-F_{AB}\mathring{L}^{2}\phi\\
&-G_{AB}^{\gamma}\mathring{L}^{2}\varphi_{\gamma}+F_{A\mathring{L}}\not{d}_{B}\mathring{L}\phi+G_{A\mathring{L}}^{\gamma}\not{d}_{B}\mathring{L}\varphi_{\gamma}+(\mathcal{FG})_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}(\mathring{L})\check{\chi}_{AB}+2(\mathcal{FG})_{\tilde{T}\mathring{L}}(\mathring{L})\check{\chi}_{AB}\\
&-(\mathcal{FG})_{B\mathring{L}}(X_{C}){\check{\chi}_{A}}^{C}-(\mathcal{FG})_{A\mathring{L}}(X_{C}){\check{\chi}_{B}^{C}}+\frac{1}{\varrho}(\mathcal{FG})_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}(\mathring{L})\not{g}_{AB}+\frac{2}{\varrho}(\mathcal{FG})_{\tilde{T}\mathring{L}}(\mathring{L})\not{g}_{AB}\\
&-\frac{1}{\varrho}(\mathcal{FG})_{B\mathring{L}}(X_{A})-\frac{1}{\varrho}(\mathcal{FG})_{A\mathring{L}}(X_{B})\big\}+{\check{\chi}_{A}}^{C}\check{\chi}_{BC}\\
&+f(\phi,\varphi,\not{d}x^{i},\mathring{L}^{i})\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathring{L}\phi\\
\not{d}\phi\\
\mathring{L}\varphi\\
\not{d}\varphi\\
\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathring{L}\phi\\
\not{d}\phi\\
\mathring{L}\varphi\\
\not{d}\varphi\\
\end{array}\right),\end{split}

(5.165)

\begin{split}\not{\mathcal{L}}_{T}\check{\chi}_{AB}=&\not{\nabla}_{AB}^{2}\mu-\frac{1}{2}\big\{\mu F_{\tilde{T}\tilde{T}}\not{\nabla}_{AB}^{2}\phi+\mu G_{\tilde{T}\tilde{T}}^{\gamma}\not{\nabla}_{AB}^{2}\varphi_{\gamma}+F_{B\mathring{L}}\not{d}_{A}T\phi+G_{B\mathring{L}}^{\gamma}\not{d}_{A}T\varphi_{\gamma}\\
&+F_{A\mathring{L}}\not{d}_{B}T\phi+G_{A\mathring{L}}^{\gamma}\not{d}_{B}T\varphi_{\gamma}-F_{AB}\mathring{L}T\phi-G_{AB}^{\gamma}\mathring{L}T\varphi_{\gamma}\big\}\\
&-\frac{1}{2}\Theta_{B}\not{d}_{A}\mu-\frac{1}{2}\Theta_{A}\not{d}_{B}\mu-\frac{1}{4}{\check{\chi}_{B}}^{C}\Upsilon_{CA}-\frac{1}{4}{\check{\chi}_{A}}^{C}\Upsilon_{CB}-\mu{\check{\chi}_{A}}^{C}\check{\chi}_{BC}+\frac{1}{\varrho^{2}}(\mu-1)\not{g}_{AB}\\
&+\frac{1}{2}\big\{\mu(\mathcal{FG})_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}(\mathring{L})+2\mu(\mathcal{FG})_{\tilde{T}\mathring{L}}(\mathring{L})-(\mathcal{FG})_{\mathring{L}\mathring{L}}(T)-(\mathcal{FG})_{\mathring{L}\tilde{T}}(T)\big\}\big(\check{\chi}_{AB}+\frac{1}{\varrho}\not{g}_{AB}\big)\\
&+\frac{1}{4\varrho}\big\{\mu(\mathcal{FG})_{B\mathring{L}}(X_{A})+\mu(\mathcal{FG})_{A\mathring{L}}(X_{B})-2\mu(\mathcal{FG})_{AB}(\mathring{L})+3\mu(\mathcal{FG})_{B\tilde{T}}(X_{A})\\
&+3\mu(\mathcal{FG})_{A\tilde{T}}(X_{B})-2(\mathcal{FG})_{AB}(T)\big\}\\
&+f_{1}(\phi,\varphi,\not{d}x^{i},\mathring{L}^{i})\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\not{d}\mathring{L}^{j}\\
\mathring{L}\phi\\
\not{d}\phi\\
\mathring{L}\varphi\\
\not{d}\varphi\\
\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mu\mathring{L}\phi\\
\mu\not{d}\phi\\
T\phi\\
\mu\mathring{L}\varphi\\
\mu\not{d}\varphi\\
T{\varphi}\\
\end{array}\right)+f_{2}(\phi,\varphi,\mathring{L}^{i})\not{\nabla}_{AB}^{2}x^{j}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mu\mathring{L}\phi\\
T\phi\\
\mu\mathring{L}\varphi\\
T{\varphi}\\
\end{array}\right).\end{split}

(5.166)

Moreover,

\begin{split}\not{\nabla}_{C}\check{\chi}_{AB}=&\not{\nabla}_{A}\check{\chi}_{CB}+F_{ij}(\not{d}_{C}\phi)\not{d}_{A}\mathring{L}^{i}(\not{d}_{B}x^{j})+G_{ij}^{\gamma}(\not{d}_{C}\varphi_{\gamma})\not{d}_{A}\mathring{L}^{i}(\not{d}_{B}x^{j})\\
&-F_{ij}(\not{d}_{A}\phi)\not{d}_{C}\mathring{L}^{i}(\not{d}_{B}x^{j})-G_{ij}^{\gamma}(\not{d}_{A}\varphi_{\gamma})\not{d}_{C}\mathring{L}^{i}(\not{d}_{B}x^{j})-g_{ij}(\not{d}_{C}\check{L}^{i})\not{\nabla}_{AB}^{2}x^{j}\\
&+g_{ij}(\not{d}_{A}\check{L}^{i})\not{\nabla}_{CB}^{2}x^{j}+\varrho^{-1}(\mathcal{FG})_{BC}(X_{A})-\varrho^{-1}(\mathcal{FG})_{AB}(X_{C})\\
&+\not{\nabla}_{A}\Lambda_{CB}-\not{\nabla}_{C}\Lambda_{AB},\end{split}

(5.167)

where
\Lambda_{AB}
is defined in (
4.96
),

\displaystyle\Theta_{A}=

\displaystyle(\mathcal{FG})_{\mathring{L}\tilde{T}}(X_{A})+(\mathcal{FG})_{\tilde{T}\tilde{T}}(X_{A})-(\mathcal{FG})_{A\tilde{T}}(\mathring{L}),

(5.168)

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{AB}=

\displaystyle\mu\big\{(\mathcal{FG})_{A\mathring{L}}(X_{B})+(\mathcal{FG})_{A\tilde{T}}(X_{B})-(\mathcal{FG})_{AB}(\mathring{L})+2(\mathcal{FG})_{B\mathring{L}}(X_{A})\big\}-(\mathcal{FG})_{AB}(T),

(5.169)

and
f,f_{1},f_{2}
are generic smooth functions of their arguments.

Proof.

(1)

By (
5.132
), one can write
\mathscr{R}_{\mathring{L}A\mathring{L}B}
as

\begin{split}\mathscr{R}_{\mathring{L}A\mathring{L}B}&=g\big(\mathscr{D}_{A}(\mathscr{D}_{\mathring{L}}\mathring{L})-\mathscr{D}_{\mathring{L}}(\mathscr{D}_{A}\mathring{L}),X_{B}\big).\end{split}

(5.170)

Substituting the expression of
\mathscr{D}_{\mathring{L}}\mathring{L}
and
\mathscr{D}_{A}\mathring{L}
in Lemma
4.5
into
(
5.170
) yields

\mathscr{R}_{\mathring{L}A\mathring{L}B}=\mu^{-1}(\mathring{L}\mu){\chi}_{AB}-\mathring{L}{\chi}_{AB}+{{\chi}_{A}}^{C}{\chi}_{BC}.

(5.171)

Inserting (
5.134
) and (
4.86
) into (
5.171
) yields (
5.163
).
On the other hand, (
5.165
) follows directly from
{\check{\chi}}_{AB}={\chi}_{AB}-\frac{1}{\varrho}\not{g}_{AB}

and
\mathring{L}\not{g}_{AB}=2{\chi}_{AB}
.

(2)

It follows from the definition for Lie derivative that

\not{\mathcal{L}}_{T}{\chi}_{AB}=\mathcal{L}_{T}{\chi}_{AB}=g(\mathscr{D}_{A}\mathscr{D}_{T}\mathring{L},X_{B})+g(\mathscr{D}_{A}\mathring{L},\mathscr{D}_{T}X_{B})-g(X_{A},\mathscr{D}_{[T,X_{B}]}\mathring{L})-\mathscr{R}_{TA\mathring{L}B},

(5.172)

where

\begin{split}&g(\mathscr{D}_{A}\mathscr{D}_{T}\mathring{L},X_{B})=g\big(\mathscr{D}_{A}(-\mathring{L}\mu\mathring{L}+{\eta}^{C}X_{C}),X_{B}\big)\\
=&\not{\nabla}^{2}_{AB}\mu+\not{\nabla}_{A}(\mu{\zeta}_{B})-(\mathring{L}\mu){\chi}_{AB}\end{split}

(5.173)

and

\begin{split}g(\mathscr{D}_{A}\mathring{L},\mathscr{D}_{T}X_{B})=&-\zeta_{A}g(\mathring{L},\mathscr{D}_{T}X_{B})+{\chi_{A}}^{D}g(X_{D},\mathscr{D}_{T}X_{B})\\
&=\zeta_{A}\eta_{B}+g(\mathscr{D}_{\not{\Pi}\mathscr{D}_{T}X_{B}}\mathring{L},X_{A})\\
&=\zeta_{A}\eta_{B}+g(\mathscr{D}_{[T,X_{B}]}\mathring{L},X_{A})+g(\mathscr{D}_{\not{\Pi}\mathscr{D}_{B}T}\mathring{L},X_{A})\\
&=\zeta_{A}\eta_{B}+g(\mathscr{D}_{[T,X_{B}]}\mathring{L},X_{A})+\mu{\sigma_{B}}^{C}g(\mathscr{D}_{C}\mathring{L},X_{A}),\end{split}

(5.174)

where (
4.100
) has been used. Due to
\not{\mathcal{L}}_{T}{\chi}_{AB}=\not{\mathcal{L}}_{T}{\chi}_{BA}
,
substituting (
5.173
) and (
5.174
) into (
5.172
) yields

\begin{split}\not{\mathcal{L}}_{T}\chi_{AB}=&\not{\nabla}_{AB}^{2}\mu+\frac{1}{2}\not{\nabla}_{A}(\mu\zeta_{B})+\frac{1}{2}\not{\nabla}_{B}(\mu\zeta_{A})-(\mathring{L}\mu)\chi_{AB}+\frac{1}{2}\zeta_{A}\eta_{B}+\frac{1}{2}\zeta_{B}\eta_{A}\\
&+\frac{1}{2}\mu{\sigma_{B}}^{C}g(\mathscr{D}_{C}\mathring{L},X_{A})+\frac{1}{2}\mu{\sigma_{A}}^{C}g(\mathscr{D}_{C}\mathring{L},X_{B})-\frac{1}{2}\mathscr{R}_{TA\mathring{L}B}-\frac{1}{2}\mathscr{R}_{TB\mathring{L}A}.\end{split}

This gives (
5.164
) with the help of (
4.97
), (
4.86
), (
4.98
), (
5.133
) and (
4.77
).
Analogously to the proof of (
5.165
), one can apply
{\check{\chi}}_{AB}={\chi}_{AB}-\frac{1}{\varrho}\not{g}_{AB}

and
\not{\mathcal{L}}_{T}\not{g}_{AB}=2\mu\sigma_{AB}
to obtain (
5.166
).

(3)

(
4.94
) implies

{\chi}_{AB}=g_{ij}\not{d}_{B}x^{j}(\not{g}^{CD}{\chi}_{AD}\not{d}_{C}x^{i})=g_{ij}\not{d}_{B}x^{j}(\not{d}_{A}\mathring{L}^{i})-\Lambda_{AB}.

(5.175)

Taking
\not{\nabla}^{B}
on both hand sides of (
5.175
) yields (
5.167
).

∎

It follows from (
5.165
) that
\check{\chi}
can be estimated by integrating along integral curves of
\mathring{L}
as soon as the right hand side of
(
5.165
) is estimated.

Proposition 5.1
.

Under the assumptions
(\star)
, when
\delta>0
is small, it holds that

|\check{\chi}|\lesssim M\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-2}.

(5.176)

Meanwhile,

|\chi|^{2}=\frac{3}{\varrho^{2}}+O(M\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-3}).

(5.177)

Proof.

Note that

|\check{\chi}|^{2}=\not{g}^{AB}\not{g}^{CD}\check{\chi}_{AC}\check{\chi}_{BD}.

Then

\mathring{L}\big(\varrho^{4}|\check{\chi}|^{2}\big)=-4\varrho^{4}\check{\chi}^{AB}\not{g}^{CD}\check{\chi}_{AC}\check{\chi}_{BD}+2\varrho^{4}(\mathring{L}\check{\chi}_{AB})\check{\chi}^{AB}.

(5.178)

Substituting (
5.165
) into (
5.178
), and using
(\star)
, (
5.118
) and (
5.119
) to estimate
the right hand side of (
5.178
) except
\check{\chi}
itself, one arrives at

|\mathring{L}\big(\varrho^{4}|\check{\chi}|^{2}\big)|\lesssim\varrho^{4}\big\{|\check{\chi}|^{3}+\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-7/2}M|\check{\chi}|+\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-5/2}M|\check{\chi}|^{2}\big\}.

This shows that for small
\delta>0
,

|\check{\chi}|\lesssim M\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-2}.

∎

At the end of this section, we point out that the operator
R_{ij}
is equivalent to the scaling operator
r\not{\nabla}
under
the assumptions
(\star)
and the estimate (
5.176
) in the following sense.

Corollary 5.1
.

Under the assumptions
(\star)
, when
\delta>0
is small,

(1)

if
\xi
is a 1-form on
S_{s,u}
, then

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j=1}^{4}(\xi_{a}R_{ij}^{a})^{2}\sim r^{2}|\xi|^{2},

(5.179)

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j=1}^{4}|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R_{ij}}\xi|^{2}\sim r^{2}|\not{\nabla}\xi|^{2}+|\xi|^{2};

(5.180)

(2)

if
\xi
is a 2-form on
S_{s,u}
, then

\begin{split}\sum_{i,j=1}^{4}|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R_{ij}}\xi|^{2}+(\textrm{tr}\xi)^{2}\sim r^{2}|\not{\nabla}\xi|^{2}+|\xi|^{2}\end{split}

(5.181)

and

|\not{\nabla}^{2}\xi|\lesssim\varrho^{-2}|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{\leq 2}\xi|.

(5.182)

This is similar to Lemma 12.22 in
[
39
]
or
in Sect.3.3.3 of
[
36
]
.

6.
L^{\infty}
estimates on the higher order derivatives of
(\phi,\partial\phi)
and some other quantities near
C_{0}

With the structure equations for
\varphi_{\gamma}
and
\phi
in the frame
\{\mathring{L},\mathring{\underline{L}},R_{ij}\}
already derived in Section
5
(see (
5.128
) and (
5.130
) respectively), we are going to close the bootstrap assumption
(\star)
by improving up to the
(N-1)^{th}
order derivatives of
\varphi_{\gamma}
and
\phi
.
To this end, we start with some preliminary results which deal only with the rotational vector fields on
S_{s,u}
.

Lemma 6.1
.

Under the assumptions
(\star)
, when
\delta>0
is small, it holds that for
k\leq N-1
,

\begin{split}&|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k+1}\not{d}x^{j}|\lesssim 1,\quad|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k}\check{\chi}|\lesssim M\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-2},\quad|R^{k+1}\mathring{L}^{j}|\lesssim 1,\\
&|R^{k+1}\check{L}^{j}|\lesssim M\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-1},\quad|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(R)}\not{\pi}|\lesssim M\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-1},\quad|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(R)}\not{\pi}_{\mathring{L}}|\lesssim M\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-1},\\
&|R^{k+1}\check{\varrho}|\lesssim M\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-1},\quad|R^{k+1}\upsilon_{ij}|\lesssim M\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}},\quad|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k+1}R|\lesssim s,\end{split}

(6.183)

where
R\in\{R_{ij}:i,j=1,2,3,4\}
.

Proof.

This will be proved by induction with respect to
k
.

(1)

When
k=0
, the estimates for
\check{\chi}
,
R\mathring{L}^{j}
,

R\check{L}^{j}
,
{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(R)}\not{\pi}
,
{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(R)}\not{\pi}_{\mathring{L}}
,
R\check{\varrho}

and
R\upsilon_{ij}
can be obtained easily from (
5.176
), (
5.119
), (
5.179
), (
4.95
),
(
4.83
), (
5.121
)
and (
4.91
), respectively.

For
RR_{ij}x^{a}
, one has

RR_{ij}x^{a}=R({\Omega_{ij}}^{a}-\upsilon_{ij}\tilde{T}^{a}),

which means
|RR_{ij}x^{a}|\lesssim s
by (
5.123
), and hence

|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}\not{d}x^{a}|=|\not{d}Rx^{a}|\lesssim r^{-1}\sum_{i,j=1}^{4}|R_{ij}Rx^{a}|\lesssim 1

(6.184)

if
\xi=\not{d}Rx^{a}
in (
5.179
) is chosen.

Next we estimate
\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R_{ij}}R_{ab}=[R_{ij},R_{ab}]
.
Due to

\begin{split}R_{ij}{R_{ab}}^{c}=&R_{ij}({\Omega_{ab}}^{c}-\upsilon_{ab}\tilde{T}^{c})\\
=&\delta_{b}^{c}({\Omega_{ij}}^{a}-\upsilon_{ij}\tilde{T}^{a})-\delta_{a}^{c}({\Omega_{ij}}^{b}-\upsilon_{ij}\tilde{T}^{b})-(R_{ij}\upsilon_{ab})\tilde{T}^{c}-\upsilon_{ab}R_{ij}\tilde{T}^{c},\end{split}

then
\not{\Pi}[R_{ij},R_{ab}]=[R_{ij},R_{ab}]
implies

\begin{split}\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R_{ij}}R_{ab}=&[R_{ij},R_{ab}]=\not{\Pi}_{c}^{d}[R_{ij},R_{ab}]^{c}\partial_{d}=\not{\Pi}_{c}^{d}(R_{ij}{R_{ab}}^{c}-R_{ab}{R_{ij}}^{c})\partial_{d}\\
=&\delta_{j}^{a}R_{ib}-\delta_{i}^{a}R_{jb}-\delta_{j}^{b}R_{ia}+\delta_{i}^{b}R_{ja}-\upsilon_{ij}\tilde{T}^{a}g_{bc}(\not{d}^{A}x^{c})X_{A}+\upsilon_{ij}\tilde{T}^{b}g_{ac}(\not{d}^{A}x^{c})X_{A}\\
&+\upsilon_{ab}\tilde{T}^{i}g_{jc}(\not{d}^{A}x^{c})X_{A}-\upsilon_{ab}\tilde{T}^{j}g_{ic}(\not{d}^{A}x^{c})X_{A}-\upsilon_{ab}(R_{ij}\tilde{T}^{c})g_{cd}(\not{d}^{A}x^{d})X_{A}\\
&+\upsilon_{ij}(R_{ab}\tilde{T}^{c})g_{cd}(\not{d}^{A}x^{d})X_{A}\end{split}

(6.185)

with the help of
\not{\Pi}_{c}^{d}\partial_{d}=g_{cd}(\not{d}^{A}x^{d})X_{A}
.
Thus,
|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R_{ij}}R_{ab}|\lesssim s
holds.

(2)

Assume that (
6.183
) holds for the orders less than or equal to
k-1

(1\leq k\leq N-1)
. We now
prove that (
6.183
) holds true for
k
.

(A)

Estimates of
\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k}\check{\chi}
and

\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(R)}\not{\pi}_{\mathring{L}}

It follows from the expression of
\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(R)}\not{\pi}_{\mathring{L}A}
in (
4.91
) and
the induction assumptions that

|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(R)}\not{\pi}_{\mathring{L}}|\lesssim s|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k}\check{\chi}|+M\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-1}.

(6.186)

Then
\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(R)}\not{\pi}_{\mathring{L}}
can be bounded as soon as
the
L^{\infty}
norm of
\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k}\check{\chi}
is derived. Then it remains to estimate
\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k}\check{\chi}
.
By
(\star)
, it follows from (
5.165
) and the induction assumptions up to the orders
k-1
that

|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k}\mathring{L}\check{\chi}|\lesssim M\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-2}|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k}\check{\chi}|+M\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-7/2}.

(6.187)

In addition, using the identity of commutator
[\mathring{L},R_{ij}]
in (
4.117
) yields

\begin{split}&\not{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathring{L}}\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k}\check{\chi}_{AB}=\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k}(\mathring{L}\check{\chi}_{AB})+[\not{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathring{L}},\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k}]\check{\chi}_{AB}\\
=&\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k}(\mathring{L}\check{\chi}_{AB})+\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}=k-1}\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k_{1}}\not{\mathcal{L}}_{[\mathring{L},R]}\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k_{2}}\check{\chi}_{AB}\\
=&\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k}(\mathring{L}\check{\chi}_{AB})+\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}=k-1}\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k_{1}}\Big\{{{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(R)}\not{\pi}_{\mathring{L}}}^{C}(\not{\nabla}_{C}\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k_{2}}\check{\chi}_{AB})\\
&+\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k_{2}}\check{\chi}_{BC}(\not{\nabla}_{A}{{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(R)}\not{\pi}_{\mathring{L}}}^{C})+\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k_{2}}\check{\chi}_{AC}(\not{\nabla}_{B}{{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(R)}\not{\pi}_{\mathring{L}}}^{C})\Big\},\end{split}

(6.188)

here the constant coefficients are neglected on the right hand of the second equality. Applying (
6.187
) and
the induction assumptions to estimate the right hand side of (
6.188
), one has

|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathring{L}}\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k}\check{\chi}|\lesssim M\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-2}|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k}\check{\chi}|+M\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-7/2}.

(6.189)

Due to

\mathring{L}\big(\varrho^{4}|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k}\check{\chi}|^{2}\big)=\varrho^{4}\big\{-4\check{\chi}^{AB}(\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k}\check{\chi}_{AC})({\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k}\check{\chi}_{B}}^{C})+2(\not{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathring{L}}\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k}\check{\chi}_{AB})(\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k}\check{\chi}^{AB})\big\},

then (
5.1
) and (
6.189
) give the estimate

|\mathring{L}(\varrho^{2}|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k}\check{\chi}|)|\lesssim M\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-2}(\varrho^{2}|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k}\check{\chi}|)+M\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-3/2}.

(6.190)

Integrating the inequality (
6.190
) along integral curves of
\mathring{L}
and applying (
6.186
) yield that for small
\delta>0
,

|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k}\check{\chi}|+s^{-1}|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(R)}\not{\pi}_{\mathring{L}}|\lesssim M\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-2}.

(B)

Estimates of
R^{k+1}\check{L}^{j}
,
R^{k+1}{\mathring{L}}^{j}
,
R^{k+1}\upsilon_{ij}
,
R^{k+1}\check{\varrho}
and
\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(R)}\not{\pi}

Since

\begin{split}R^{k}R_{ab}\check{L}^{j}=R^{k}({R_{ab}}^{A}\not{d}_{A}\check{L}^{j})\overset{\eqref{deL}}{=}&R^{k}\big\{{R_{ab}}^{A}\check{\chi}_{AB}(\not{d}^{B}x^{j})-(\mathcal{FG})_{\mathring{L}\tilde{T}}(R_{ab})\tilde{T}^{j}\\
&-\frac{1}{2}(\mathcal{FG})_{\tilde{T}\tilde{T}}(R_{ab})\tilde{T}^{j}+{R_{ab}}^{A}\Lambda_{AB}(\not{d}^{B}x^{i})\big\},\end{split}

(6.191)

this implies
|R^{k+1}\check{L}^{j}|\lesssim M\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-1}
by the induction assumptions,
(\star)
and the estimate of
\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k}\check{\chi}
in Part
(1)
. Hence
|R^{k+1}{\mathring{L}}^{j}|\lesssim 1

holds for small
\delta>0
.

The estimates for
R^{k+1}\upsilon_{ij}
and
R^{k+1}\check{\varrho}
can be obtained by (
4.83
) and (
5.121
) directly, and
|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(R)}\not{\pi}|\lesssim M\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-1}
holds by (
4.91
).

(C)

Estimate of
\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k+1}R

Taking the Lie derivatives
R^{k}
on both hand sides of (
6.185
), using the results in Part
(1)-(2)
, the induction assumptions for

k-1
and
(\star)
, one can estimate
\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k+1}R
in (
6.1
)
directly.

(D)

Estimate of
R^{k+2}x^{j}

Note that

\begin{split}R^{k+1}R_{ab}x^{j}&=R^{k+1}(\Omega_{ab}-\upsilon_{ab}\tilde{T})x^{j}=R^{k+1}(x^{a}\delta_{b}^{j}-x^{b}\delta_{a}^{j}-\upsilon_{ab}\tilde{T}^{j}).\end{split}

Then by induction assumptions up to the orders of
k-1
, the estimate of
R^{k+1}\upsilon_{ab}
and
R^{k+1}{\tilde{T}}^{j}=(-R^{k+1}g^{0j}-R^{k+1}\mathring{L}^{j})
in Part
(2)
,
|R^{k+2}x^{j}|\lesssim s
is obtained, and hence
|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k+1}\not{d}x^{j}|\lesssim 1
holds as for (
6.184
).

∎

With the help of Lemma
6.1
, the estimates for the higher order derivatives of
\mu
with the rotational
vector fields can be derived as follows.

Proposition 6.1
.

Under the same assumptions in Lemma
6.1
, it holds that

|R^{k+1}\mu|\lesssim M^{2}\delta^{1-2\varepsilon_{0}},\quad k\leq N-1.

(6.192)

Proof.

This can be proved by induction.

Note that

\mathring{L}\big(\varrho^{2}|\not{d}\mu|^{2}\big)=\varrho^{2}\big\{-2\check{\chi}^{AB}(\not{d}_{A}\mu)(\not{d}_{B}\mu)+2\not{g}^{AB}(\not{d}_{A}\mathring{L}\mu)(\not{d}_{B}\mu)\big\}.

(6.193)

Substituting (
4.86
) into (
6.193
), and applying
(\star)
, (
5.1
) and (
5.124
), one gets

|\mathring{L}\big(\varrho|\not{d}\mu|\big)|\lesssim M\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-2}\big(\varrho|\not{d}\mu|\big)+M^{2}\delta^{1-2\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-3/2}.

Hence,

|\not{d}\mu|\lesssim M^{2}\delta^{1-2\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-1},

(6.194)

which yields (
6.192
) for
k=0
.

Assume that (
6.192
) holds for the orders up to
k-1

(1\leq k\leq N-1)
. By (
4.117
), one has

\begin{split}\mathring{L}R^{k+1}\mu&=[\mathring{L},R^{k+1}]\mu+R^{k+1}\mathring{L}\mu\\
&=\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}=k}\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k_{1}}\big({\vphantom{{\not{\pi}_{\mathring{L}}}^{A}}}^{(R)}{\not{\pi}_{\mathring{L}}}^{A}\not{d}_{A}R^{k_{2}}\mu\big)+R^{k+1}\mathring{L}\mu.\end{split}

(6.195)

Using (
4.86
) and (
5.124
), the induction assumptions, Lemma
6.1
and
(\star)
to
estimate the right hand side of (
6.195
),
one can obtain

|\mathring{L}R^{k+1}\mu|\lesssim M\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-2}|R^{k+1}\mu|+M^{2}\delta^{1-2\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-3/2}.

(6.196)

Integrating (
6.196
) along integral curves of
\mathring{L}
yields that for small
\delta>0
,

|R^{k+1}\mu|\lesssim M^{2}\delta^{1-2\varepsilon_{0}}.

∎

As a consequence of Lemma
6.1
and Proposition
6.1
, it follows from the expressions of
{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(R)}\not{\pi}_{TA}
,
{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(T)}\not{\pi}_{AB}
and
{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(T)}\not{\pi}_{\mathring{L}A}

in (
4.91
) and (
4.89
) that for
k\leq N-1
,

|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(R)}\not{\pi}_{T}|\lesssim M^{2}\delta^{1-2\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-1},\quad|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(T)}\not{\pi}|\lesssim M\delta^{-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-1},\quad|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{k}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(T)}\not{\pi}_{\mathring{L}}|\lesssim M\delta^{-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-1}.

(6.197)

For derivatives involving
T
, one can get the following estimates by similar analysis for Lemma
6.1
and Proposition
6.1
.

Proposition 6.2
.

Under the assumptions
(\star)
, for any operator
\bar{Z}\in\{T,R_{ij}\}
, and suitably small
\delta>0
,
it holds that for
k\leq N-1
,

\begin{split}&|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{\bar{Z}}^{k+1;l}\not{d}x^{j}|\lesssim\delta^{1-l}s^{-1},\quad|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{\bar{Z}}^{k;l}\check{\chi}|\lesssim M\delta^{1-l-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-2},\quad|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{\bar{Z}}^{k+1;l}R|\lesssim M^{2}\delta^{2-l-2\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-1},\\
&|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{\bar{Z}}^{k;l}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(R)}\not{\pi}|\lesssim M\delta^{1-l-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-1},\quad|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{\bar{Z}}^{k;l}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(R)}\not{\pi}_{\mathring{L}}|\lesssim M\delta^{1-l-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-1},\quad|\bar{Z}^{k+1;l}\check{\varrho}|\lesssim M\delta^{1-l-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-1},\\
&|\bar{Z}^{k+1;l}\check{L}^{j}|\lesssim M\delta^{1-l-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-1},\quad|\bar{Z}^{k+1;l}\upsilon_{ij}|\lesssim M\delta^{1-l-\varepsilon_{0}},\quad|\bar{Z}^{k+1;l}\mu|\lesssim M^{2}\delta^{1-l-2\varepsilon_{0}},\\
&|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{\bar{Z}}^{k;l}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(R)}\not{\pi}_{T}|\lesssim M^{2}\delta^{1-l-2\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-1},\quad|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{\bar{Z}}^{k;l}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(T)}\not{\pi}|\lesssim M\delta^{-l-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-1},\quad|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{\bar{Z}}^{k;l}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(T)}\not{\pi}_{\mathring{L}}|\lesssim M\delta^{-l-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-1},\end{split}

(6.198)

where the array
(k;l)
means that the number of
\bar{Z}
is
k
and the number of
T
is
l
(
l\geq 1
).

Proof.

We start with the special case of
l=1
.

(1)

Taking Lie derivative
R^{p}
on both hand sides of (
5.166
),
and applying Lemma
6.1
, Proposition
6.1
and
(\star)
, one has that for
p\leq N-2
,

|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{p}\not{\mathcal{L}}_{T}\check{\chi}|\lesssim M\delta^{-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-2}.

(6.199)

By
[T,R]={\vphantom{{\not{\pi}_{T}}^{A}}}^{(R)}{\not{\pi}_{T}}^{A}X_{A}
in (
4.117
)
and

\not{\mathcal{L}}_{\bar{Z}}^{k;1}\check{\chi}=\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{p_{1}}\not{\mathcal{L}}_{T}\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{p_{2}}\check{\chi}=\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{p_{1}}\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{p_{2}}\not{\mathcal{L}}_{T}\check{\chi}+\sum_{q_{1}+q_{2}=p_{2}-1}\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{p_{1}}\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{q_{1}}\not{\mathcal{L}}_{[T,R]}\not{\mathcal{L}}_{R}^{q_{2}}\check{\chi},

then
|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{\bar{Z}}^{k;1}\check{\chi}|\lesssim M\delta^{-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-2}
is shown for
k\leq N-1

and
l=1
by making use of (
6.199
), (
6.197
) and the estimate of
\check{\chi}
in Lemma
6.1
.

(2)

Note that

\begin{split}&\not{\mathcal{L}}_{T}\not{d}x^{j}=(\not{d}\mu)\tilde{T}^{j}+\mu\not{d}\tilde{T},\\
&T\check{L}^{j}=T\mathring{L}^{j}-\frac{\mu-1}{\varrho}\tilde{T}^{j}-\frac{\check{T}^{j}}{\varrho},\\
&\not{\mathcal{L}}_{T}R=[T,R]={\vphantom{\not{\pi}_{T}}}^{(R)}\not{\pi}_{T}^{A}X_{A}.\end{split}

Following similar arguments in the part (1), one can get the estimates in (
6.198
)
for
\not{\mathcal{L}}_{\bar{Z}}^{k+1;1}\not{d}x^{j}
,
\not{\mathcal{L}}_{\bar{Z}}^{k+1;1}R
,
\bar{Z}^{k+1;1}\check{L}^{j}
,
\bar{Z}^{k+1;1}\check{\varrho}
and
\bar{Z}^{k+1;1}\upsilon_{ij}
by virtue of the equalities (
4.99
), (
5.121
) and (
4.83
).

(3)

Now we turn to the estimate of
\mu
by induction.

By (
4.117
), one has

\begin{split}\mathring{L}T\mu&=[\mathring{L},T]\mu+T\mathring{L}\mu=(-\not{d}^{A}\mu-2\mu\zeta^{A})\not{d}_{A}\mu+T\mathring{L}\mu.\end{split}

Then it follows from the equation (
4.86
),
(\star)
, (
5.124
) and (
6.192
) that

|\mathring{L}T\mu|\lesssim M^{2}\delta^{-2\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-3/2}+M\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-5/2}|T\mu|.

(6.200)

Integrating (
6.200
) along integral curves of
\mathring{L}
and applying Gronwall’s inequality yield

|T\mu|\lesssim M^{2}\delta^{-2\varepsilon_{0}}.

(6.201)

Inductively, one can assume that for
q\leq p-1
and
p\leq N-1
,

|R^{q}T\mu|\lesssim M^{2}\delta^{-2\varepsilon_{0}}.

(6.202)

Note that

\begin{split}\mathring{L}R^{p}T\mu=&\sum_{p_{1}+p_{2}=p-1}R^{p_{1}}\big({\vphantom{{\not{\pi}_{\mathring{L}}}^{A}}}^{(R)}{\not{\pi}_{\mathring{L}}}^{A}\not{d}_{A}R^{p_{2}}T\mu\big)+R^{p}\big\{(-\not{d}^{A}\mu-2\mu\zeta^{A})\not{d}_{A}\mu+T\mathring{L}\mu\big\}.\end{split}

It follows from this, (
6.202
), Lemma
6.1
and Proposition
6.1
that

|\mathring{L}R^{p}T\mu|\lesssim M\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-2}|R^{p}T\mu|+M^{2}\delta^{-2\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-3/2},

which implies
|R^{p}T\mu|\lesssim M^{2}\delta^{-2\varepsilon_{0}}
, and
hence
|\bar{Z}^{k+1;1}\mu|\lesssim M^{2}\delta^{-2\varepsilon_{0}}
. The estimates of
\not{\mathcal{L}}_{\bar{Z}}^{k;1}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(R)}\not{\pi}
,
\not{\mathcal{L}}_{\bar{Z}}^{k;1}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(T)}\not{\pi}
,

\not{\mathcal{L}}_{\bar{Z}}^{k;1}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(T)}\not{\pi}_{\mathring{L}}
and
\not{\mathcal{L}}_{\bar{Z}}^{k;1}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(R)}\not{\pi}_{\mathring{L}}
can be obtained
directly by (
4.89
) and (
4.91
).
Meanwhile, the estimate for
\not{\mathcal{L}}_{\bar{Z}}^{k;1}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(R)}\not{\pi}_{T}

follows from (
4.91
).

Analogously, if the number of the derivatives with respect to
T
in (
6.198
) is
l

(l\geq 2)
, one can utilize an induction
argument on
l
to get the estimates in (
6.198
).
∎

We now improve
L^{\infty}
estimates of some derivatives of
\varphi_{\gamma}
and
\phi

with respect to the vector field
\bar{Z}
(
\bar{Z}\in\{T,R_{ij}\}
).

Corollary 6.1
.

Under the assumptions
(\star)
, for any operator
\bar{Z}\in\{T,R_{ij}\}
and suitably small
\delta>0
, it holds that for
k\leq N-1
,

\displaystyle|\bar{Z}^{k;l}\mathring{\underline{L}}\varphi_{\gamma}(s,u,\vartheta)|+|\bar{Z}^{k;l}T\varphi_{\gamma}(s,u,\vartheta)|\lesssim\delta^{-l-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-3/2},

(6.203)

here
l
is the number of
T
in
\bar{Z}^{k}
.

Proof.

For any
p
with
p+l\leq N-1
, by Lemma
4.7
, one has

\begin{split}&R^{p}T^{l}(H_{\gamma}+\mu\not{\triangle}\varphi_{\gamma})=R^{p}T^{l}\big(\mathring{L}\mathring{\underline{L}}\varphi_{\gamma}+\frac{3}{2\varrho}\mathring{\underline{L}}\varphi_{\gamma}\big)\\
=&\mathring{L}\big(R^{p}T^{l}\mathring{\underline{L}}\varphi_{\gamma}\big)+\frac{3}{2\varrho}R^{p}T^{l}\mathring{\underline{L}}\varphi_{\gamma}-\sum_{p_{1}+p_{2}=p-1}R^{p_{1}}\big({\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}^{(R)}\not{\pi}_{\mathring{L}}^{A}\not{d}_{A}R^{p_{2}}T^{l}\mathring{\underline{L}}\varphi_{\gamma}\big)\\
&-\sum_{l_{1}+l_{2}=l-1}R^{p}T^{l_{1}}\big({\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}^{(T)}\not{\pi}_{\mathring{L}}^{A}\not{d}_{A}T^{l_{2}}\mathring{\underline{L}}\varphi_{\gamma}\big)+\sum_{l_{1}+l_{2}=l,l_{1}\geq 1}T^{l_{1}}(\frac{3}{2\varrho})\cdot R^{p}T^{l_{2}}\mathring{\underline{L}}\varphi_{\gamma}.\end{split}

(6.204)

Note that the terms in the last three summations on the right hand side of (
6.204
) can be estimated by Lemma
6.1
and Proposition
6.2
.
In addition, one can estimate
R^{p}T^{l}H_{\gamma}

by the expression of
H_{\gamma}
in (
5.129
). Then it is derived from (
6.204
) that

\begin{split}|\mathring{L}\big(\varrho^{3/2}R^{p}T^{l}\mathring{\underline{L}}\varphi_{\gamma}\big)|\lesssim M^{2}\delta^{1-l-2\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-2}.\end{split}

(6.205)

Subsequently, integrating (
6.205
) along the integral
curves of
\mathring{L}
yields

|\varrho^{3/2}R^{p}T^{l}\mathring{\underline{L}}\varphi_{\gamma}(s,u,\vartheta)-{\varrho_{0}}^{3/2}R^{p}T^{l}\mathring{\underline{L}}\varphi_{\gamma}(t_{0},u,\vartheta)|\lesssim M^{2}\delta^{1-l-2\varepsilon_{0}}.

This implies that for small
\delta>0
,

|R^{p}T^{l}\mathring{\underline{L}}\varphi_{\gamma}(s,u,\vartheta)|\lesssim\delta^{-l-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-3/2}.

(6.206)

Thus, (
6.203
) follows from (
6.206
) and
\mathring{\underline{L}}=2T+\mu\mathring{L}
.
∎

To further improve the estimate of
\varphi_{\gamma}
, based on (
6.203
) and

T=\frac{\partial}{\partial u}-\Xi^{A}X_{A}
, it is necessary to treat the components of
\Xi
.

Lemma 6.2
.

Under the assumptions
(\star)
, for suitably small
\delta>0
, it then holds

|\Xi|\lesssim M\delta^{-\varepsilon_{0}}s.

(6.207)

Proof.

Note that
\Xi
is a vector field on
S_{s,u}
and one has

\mathring{L}\Xi^{A}=[T,\mathring{L}]^{A}=\big(\mathscr{D}_{T}\mathring{L}-\mathscr{D}_{\mathring{L}}T\big)^{A}=\not{d}^{A}\mu+2\mu\zeta^{A}.

(6.208)

Then

\mathring{L}(\varrho^{-2}|\Xi|^{2})=\varrho^{-2}\big\{2\check{\chi}_{AB}\Xi^{A}\Xi^{B}+2\not{g}_{AB}(\not{d}^{A}\mu+2\mu\zeta^{A})\Xi^{B}\big\}.

This, together with the estimates (
6.194
), (
5.176
),
(\star)
and (
4.97
), yields

|\mathring{L}(\varrho^{-1}|\Xi|)|\lesssim\varrho^{-1}\big\{M\delta^{1-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-2}|\Xi|+M\delta^{-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-1}\big\}.

(6.209)

Thus (
6.207
) follows immediately from integrating (
6.209
) along integral curves of
\mathring{L}
.
∎

Corollary 6.2
.

Under the assumptions
(\star)
with suitably small
\delta>0
, it holds that for
k\leq N-1
,

\displaystyle|\bar{Z}^{k;l}\varphi_{\gamma}(s,u,\vartheta)|\lesssim\delta^{1-l-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-3/2},

(6.210)

\displaystyle|\bar{Z}^{k;l}\phi(s,u,\vartheta)|\lesssim\delta^{2-l-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-3/2}.

(6.211)

Proof.

Notice that (
6.203
) implies

|T\bar{Z}^{k;l}\varphi_{\gamma}(s,u,\vartheta)|\lesssim\delta^{-l-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-3/2}.

(6.212)

Substituting
T=\frac{\partial}{\partial u}-\Xi^{A}X_{A}
into (
6.212
), and using
(\star)
and (
6.207
), one has

\displaystyle|\frac{\partial}{\partial u}\bar{Z}^{k;l}\varphi_{\gamma}(s,u,\vartheta)|\lesssim\delta^{-\varepsilon_{0}-l}s^{-3/2}.

(6.213)

Integrating (
6.213
) from
0
to
u
leads to the estimate of
\bar{Z}^{k;l}\varphi_{\gamma}
in (
6.210
) since
\bar{Z}^{k;l}\varphi_{\gamma}
vanishes on
C_{0}^{s}
.

Next we derive the estimate (
6.211
). Note that

|\bar{Z}^{k;l}T\phi|=|\bar{Z}^{k;l}\big(\mu\tilde{T}^{j}\varphi_{j}\big)|\lesssim\delta^{1-l-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-3/2}.

Then by the bootstrap assumption
(\star)
for
\phi
, one has
|T\bar{Z}^{k;l}\phi|\lesssim\delta^{1-l-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-3/2},

which implies

|\frac{\partial}{\partial u}\bar{Z}^{k;l}\phi|\lesssim\delta^{1-l-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-3/2}.

(6.214)

Thus (
6.211
) holds after integrating (
6.214
) from
0
to
u
.
∎

It is noticed that the results in Proposition
6.2
only involve only the vectorfields
\{T,R_{ij}\}
.
We next deal with the corresponding quantities involving the remaining scaling vectorfield
\varrho\mathring{L}
in the frame

\{\varrho\mathring{L},T,R_{ij}\}
. In fact, there are
explicit expressions of
\check{\chi}
and
\varrho\check{L}^{i}
under the action of
\mathring{L}
(see (
5.165
) and (
4.93
)),
then by taking the Lie derivative of
\varrho\mathring{L}\check{\chi}
with respect to
\bar{Z}
, one obtains the estimate
of
\not{\mathcal{L}}_{\bar{Z}}^{k}\not{\mathcal{L}}_{\varrho\mathring{L}}\check{\chi}
as in (
6.199
).
On the other hand, the terms containing the derivatives of
\varrho\mathring{L}
can be estimated similarly as in the proof of Proposition
6.2
, we omit the tedious analysis and
give the conclusions directly in the following proposition:

Proposition 6.3
.

Under the assumptions
(\star)
with suitably small
\delta>0
, for any operate
Z\in\{\varrho\mathring{L},T,R_{ij}\}
,
it holds that for
k\leq N-1
,

\begin{split}&|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{Z}^{k+1;l,m}\not{d}x^{j}|\lesssim\delta^{-l},\quad|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{Z}^{k;l,m}\check{\chi}|\lesssim M\delta^{1-l-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-2},\quad|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{Z}^{k+1;l,m}R|\lesssim M\delta^{1-l-\varepsilon_{0}},\\
&|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{Z}^{k;l,m}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(R)}\not{\pi}|\lesssim M\delta^{1-l-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-1},\quad|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{Z}^{k;l,m}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(R)}\not{\pi}_{\mathring{L}}|\lesssim M\delta^{1-l-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-1},\quad|Z^{k+1;l,m}\check{\varrho}|\lesssim M\delta^{1-l-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-1},\\
&|Z^{k+1;l,m}\check{L}^{j}|\lesssim M\delta^{1-l-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-1},\quad|Z^{k+1;l,m}\upsilon_{ij}|\lesssim M\delta^{1-l-\varepsilon_{0}},\quad|Z^{k+1;l,m}\mu|\lesssim M^{2}\delta^{1-l-2\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-1},\\
&|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{Z}^{k;l,m}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(R)}\not{\pi}_{T}|\lesssim M^{2}\delta^{1-l-2\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-1},\quad|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{Z}^{k;l,m}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(T)}\not{\pi}|\lesssim M\delta^{-l-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-1},\quad|\not{\mathcal{L}}_{Z}^{k;l,m}{\vphantom{\not{\pi}}}{}^{(T)}\not{\pi}_{\mathring{L}}|\lesssim M\delta^{-l-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-1},\end{split}

(6.215)

where the array
(k;l,m)
means that the number of
Z
is
k
, the number of
T
is
l
, and the number
of
\varrho\mathring{L}
is
m
(
m\geq 1
).

(
6.203
) implies that
|R^{p}T^{l}\mathring{\underline{L}}\varphi_{\gamma}(s,u,\vartheta)|\lesssim\delta^{-l-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-3/2}

for
p+l\leq N-1
. Assume that the following induction assumptions hold:

|R^{p}T^{l}(\varrho\mathring{L})^{m-1}\mathring{\underline{L}}\varphi_{\gamma}(s,u,\vartheta)|\lesssim\delta^{-l-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-3/2}\quad\text{for $p+l+m-1\leq N-1$ and $m\geq 1$}.

(6.216)

It follows from (
5.128
) that

R^{p}T^{l}(\varrho\mathring{L})^{m}\mathring{\underline{L}}\varphi_{\gamma}=R^{p}T^{l}(\varrho\mathring{L})^{m-1}\big(-\frac{3}{2}\mathring{\underline{L}}\varphi_{\gamma}+\varrho H_{\gamma}+\varrho\mu\not{\triangle}\varphi_{\gamma}\big).

(6.217)

According to the explicit expression (
5.129
) of
H_{\gamma}
, the second term in the bracket of the right hand side of (
6.217
)
can be estimated easily by (
6.183
), (
6.2
) or (
6.215
). This, together with
the assumption (
6.216
), implies that when
p+l+m\leq N
,

|R^{p}T^{l}(\varrho\mathring{L})^{m}\mathring{\underline{L}}\varphi_{\gamma}|\lesssim\delta^{-l-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-3/2}.

(6.218)

This yields
|\frac{\partial}{\partial u}R^{p}T^{l}(\varrho\mathring{L})^{m}\varphi_{\gamma}|\lesssim\delta^{-l-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-3/2}
by the
same argument as for (
6.213
).
Thus, for
p+l+m\leq N
and
m\geq 1
,

|R^{p}T^{l}(\varrho\mathring{L})^{m}\varphi_{\gamma}|\lesssim\delta^{1-l-\varepsilon_{0}}s^{-3/2}.

(6.219)

Using Lemma
4.7
and rearranging the orders of derivatives in (
6.218
) and (
6.219
), one can get

Corollary 6.3
.

Under the assumptions
(\star)
with suitably small
\delta>0
, for
Z\in\{\varrho\mathring{L},T,R_{ij}\}
and
k\leq N-1
, it then holds that
